Generalized Symmetries From Fusion Actions
Chongying Dong, Siu-Hung Ng, Li Ren, Feng Xu

Abstract
. Let A A be a condensable algebra in a modular tensor category 𝒞 \mathcal{C} . We define an action of the fusion category 𝒞 A \mathcal{C}_{A} of A A -modules in 𝒞 \mathcal{C} on the morphism space Hom 𝒞 ​ ( x , A ) \mbox{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(x,A) for any x x in 𝒞 \mathcal{C} , whose characters are generalized Frobenius-Schur indicators. This fusion action can be considered on A A , and we prove a categorical generalization of Schur-Weyl duality for this action. For any fusion subcategory ℬ \mathcal{B} of 𝒞 A \mathcal{C}_{A} containing all the local A A -modules, we prove the invariant subobject B = A ℬ B=A^{\mathcal{B}} is a condensable subalgebra of A A . The assignment of ℬ \mathcal{B} to A ℬ A^{\mathcal{B}} defines a Galois correspondence between this kind of fusion subcategories of 𝒞 A \mathcal{C}_{A} and the condensable subalgebras of A A . In the context of VOA, we prove for any nice VOAs U ⊂ A U\subset A , U = A 𝒞 A U=A^{\mathcal{C}_{A}} where 𝒞 = ℳ U \mathcal{C}=\mathcal{M}_{U} is the U U -module category. In particular, if U = A G U=A^{G} for some finite automorphism group G G of A , A, the fusion action of 𝒞 A \mathcal{C}_{A} on A A is equivalent to the G G -action on A . A.



1.
Introduction

Motivated by orbifold theory, we explore the notion of fusion actions on condensible algebras within a modular tensor category in this paper.

Orbifold theory studies the action of a group
G
on a vertex operator algebra
A
. We highlight three key results from orbifold theory that are relevant to this paper. The first is the Schur-Weyl duality
DLMcomp1996
;
KT1997
.
Assume that
A
is a simple VOA and
G
is a compact Lie group acting on
A
continuously. Then
A

decomposes into

A=\bigoplus_{W\in\operatorname{Irr}(G)}W\otimes A_{W}

where
W
is the finite dimensional irreducible module and
A_{W}=\operatorname{Hom}_{G}(W,A)
is the multiplicity space.
Then
A^{G}=\operatorname{Hom}_{G}(\mathbb{C},A)
is a simple vertex operator subalgebra of
A
and each
A_{W}
is a (nonzero) irreducible

A^{G}
-module. Moreover
A_{W_{1}}
and
A_{W_{2}}
are isomorphic iff
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
are isomorphic.
The second one is that if
G
is a finite group then
H\mapsto A^{H}
gives a one to one correspondence between subgroups of
G
and
subalgebras of
A
which contain
A^{G}

DM1997
;
HMT1999
. Finally, if we further assume that
A^{G}
is rational,
C_{2}
-cofinite and the weight of any twisted module is positive except
A
itself
then
\dim_{A^{G}}A^{H}=o(G)/o(H)
for any subgroup
H

DJX2013
. These assumptions on
A^{G}
ensure that
\dim_{A^{G}}A^{H}
is well defined and computable.

It is well known that not every subalgebra
U
of
A
can be realized as
A^{G}
for some automorphism group
G.
A natural question arises: can
U
be realized as the fixed-point subalgebra under the action of something analogous to a group? To answer this question, we have to assume that
U
is rational and
C_{2}
-cofinite. In this case the
U
-module category
{\mathcal{M}_{U}}
is a modular tensor category (MTC)
huang2005vertex
and
A
is a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{M}_{U}}

HKL
. We provide a positive answer to this question by employing the categorical Schur-Weyl duality, which is proved in this paper. Our approach to Galois correspondence is partly motivated by
Xu2014
on conformal nets.

We now state our results in details. Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a modular tensor category (MTC) and
A
a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}.
Then

A=\bigoplus_{x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}W_{x}\otimes x

where
W_{x}=\operatorname{Hom}_{{\mathcal{C}}}(x,A).
It follows from
KO02
that
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
is an MTC and
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
is a spherical fusion category. The fusion algebra
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
over
\mathbb{C}
is well-known to be a semisimple associative algebra. Set

e_{\mathds{1}}=\frac{1}{\dim({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})}\sum_{X\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})}d_{A}(X)X.

Then
e_{1}
is a primitive central idempotent of
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})

and
(1-e_{\mathds{1}})K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
is a semisimple ideal of
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A}).

We define an action of the fusion category
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
on
W_{x}
for any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
in this paper, and we write
Xf
for the action of
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on
f\in W_{x}
. We can define
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
-invariants

W_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=\{f\in W_{x}\mid Xf=d_{A}(X)f\ {\rm for}\ X\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}\}

and
A^{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=\sum_{x}W_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}x
as an object in
{\mathcal{C}}
, and will be shown to inherit the algebra structure of
A
in Section
5
.

Our first main result is the following Schur-Weyl duality.

Theorem I
.

Let
A
be a condensable algebra in any modular tensor category
{\mathcal{C}}
. Then

(i)

The kernel of the action of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on
W_{A}
is equal to
(1-e_{\mathds{1}})K({\mathcal{C}}_{A}).

(ii)

W_{x}
is an irreducible
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-module for any
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
whenever
W_{x}\neq 0
.

(iii)

For any
x,y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
,
W_{x}\cong W_{y}
as
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-module if and only if
x=y
.

(iv)

The
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
-action defines an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-algebras
e_{\mathds{1}}K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
.

Although the duality result for the fusion category action on
A
resembles the duality result in orbifold theory,
the proof in
DLMcomp1996
for orbifold theory setting cannot be extended to fusion action on a VOA. In the case of orbifold theory of a finite automorphism group
G
of the VOA
A
,
A
is a semisimple
G
-module and the key step involves analyzing the multiplicity
spaces
A_{W}
for irreducible
G
-modules
W
. In contrast, in our categorical setting,
A
is a condensable algebra in an MTC
{\mathcal{C}}
. The relevant multiplicity spaces are
W_{x}
of
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}).
The generalized Frobenius-Schur
indicators
NS10
play a crucial role in defining the
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
-action on
W_{x}
and in the proof of Theorem I.

Note that the algebra
e_{\mathds{1}}K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})=e_{\mathds{1}}K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})e_{\mathds{1}}
can also be interpreted in terms of hypergroup
Bi2017
. In fact,
e_{\mathds{1}}K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
is the quotient of hypergroup
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
by
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}).

We also show that the action of a finite group
G
on a VOA
A
is a special case of a fusion category action, under the assumption that
A^{G}
is rational and
C_{2}
-cofinite. In this case
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{M}_{A^{G}}}
is the
A^{G}
-module category and
e_{\mathds{1}}K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}[G].
Moreover, the action of
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
recovers the action of
G
on
A.

As in the orbifold theory, we then use the duality result to give a Galois correspondence from the fusion action. Let
{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
be the set of fusion categories of
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
containing
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
and

{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
be the set of subalgebras of
A.

Theorem II
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a pseudounitary modular tensor category, and
A
a condensable subalgebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
. Then the assignment
{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}\to{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
,
{\mathcal{B}}\mapsto A^{\mathcal{B}}
, defines an order-reversing bijection, whose inverse is given by assignment
{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}\to{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
,
(B,\iota)\mapsto({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}
, where
(B,\iota)
is defined in Section 5. In particular,
{\mathcal{B}}=({\mathcal{C}}_{A^{\mathcal{B}}}^{0})_{A}
for any
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
and

\dim({\mathcal{B}})=\frac{\dim({\mathcal{C}})}{d(A)d(B)}\,.

Moreover,
A^{{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}}=A
and
A^{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}={\mathds{1}}
which is the unit object of
{\mathcal{C}}.

A hypergroup action in the categorical setting was previously introduced by Riesen in
Rie2025
, and some results of
Xu2013
;
Xu2014
;
Bi2017
on conformal nets are extended to vertex operator algebras. In particular, he proved that hypergroup action preserves duality. Moreover, if
A
is an extension of rational VOA
V
, there is an hypergroup
K
acting on
A
such that
A^{K}=V.
This is closely related to our result that
A^{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}={\mathds{1}}.

We would like to point out that such a correspondence was previously established in
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.1)
via the theory of relative center for
{\mathcal{C}}=Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
or
A
is the Lagrangian algebra of
{\mathcal{C}}
. In summary of
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.1)
, the subalgebra
B
of
A
corresponding a fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is given by
B=I({\mathds{1}})
, where
I
is the right adjoint of the forgetful functor
F_{\mathcal{B}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
, the relative center of
{\mathcal{B}}
in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}.
In contrast, our approach follows the classical notion of Galois correspondence using invariance subspaces of fusion actions and relies crucially on the Schur-Weyl duality established in Theorem I. Connections between these two approaches will be discussed in details in Section
6
.

Now, we elaborate the preceding categorical results in the context of vertex operator algebras. Let
U
be a simple, rational,
C_{2}
-cofinite VOA of CFT type such that the weight of any irreducible module is positive except
U
itself. Then the
U
-module category
{\mathcal{M}_{U}}
is a pseudounitary modular tensor category. Let
A
be simple VOA which contains
U
as a subVOA. Then
A\in{\mathcal{M}_{U}}
is a condensable algebra which has a decomposition

A=\bigoplus_{x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}_{U}})}W_{x}\otimes x

as before. Applying our results to
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{M}_{U}}
and

A
, we have the following theorem.

Theorem III
.

Consider the categorical fusion category
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
on
A
. We have

(i)

W_{x}
is an irreducible
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
-module for any
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
whenever
W_{x}\neq 0
.

(ii)

For any
x,y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
,
W_{x}\cong W_{y}
as
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
-module if and only if
x=y
.

(iii)

The restriction
e_{\mathds{1}}K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{U}(A,A)
is an isomorphism of algebras,

(iv)

{\mathcal{B}}\mapsto A^{\mathcal{B}}
gives
a one to one correspondence between the fusion subcategories of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
containing
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
and subVOAs of
A
containing
U.
In particular,
A^{{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}}=A
and

A^{{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}}=U.

Acknowledgement:
We would like to thank Terry Gannon for some discussions related to this paper. S.-H. N. was partially supported by the Simons Foundation MPS-TSM-00008039.

2.
Basic terminologies on fusion categories

In this section, some basic terminologies, conventions and results on fusion categories are provided for the rest this paper. The readers are referred to
EGNO
for more details.

2.1.
Conventions and terminologies

Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be a semisimple
\mathbb{C}
-linear category. We denote by
\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})
a complete set of nonisomorphic simple objects of
{\mathcal{M}}
. Following the conventions in
MacLane
, the morphism space of
{\mathcal{M}}
from
x
to
y
is simply abbreviated as
{\mathcal{M}}(x,y)
, instead of
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}}(x,y)
, and
[x,y]_{\mathcal{M}}:=\dim_{\mathbb{C}}{\mathcal{M}}(x,y)
.

Throughout this paper, a fusion category
{\mathcal{M}}
is a semisimple
\mathbb{C}
-linear rigid finite tensor category with finite-dimensional hom-spaces and
\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})
is a finite set containing the unit object
{\mathds{1}}
. The left dual object of any object
x\in{\mathcal{M}}
is denoted by
x^{*}
. The duality of a fusion category
{\mathcal{M}}
can be extended to a contravariant tensor functor
(-)^{*}
from
{\mathcal{M}}
to its reversed tensor category
{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
. A fusion subcategory of
{\mathcal{M}}
always means a full subcategory which is closed under the tensor product of
{\mathcal{M}}
.

The Grothendieck ring
K_{0}({\mathcal{M}})
of a fusion category
{\mathcal{M}}
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{+}
-based ring. In particular,
K_{0}({\mathcal{M}})
an algebra over
\mathbb{Z}
with the canonical basis
\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})
. In this paper, we often needs to deal with the
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
K({\mathcal{M}}):=K({\mathcal{M}}){\otimes}_{\mathbb{Z}}\mathbb{C}
. It is well-known that
K({\mathcal{M}})
is a semisimple
\mathbb{C}
-algebra.

A fusion category
{\mathcal{M}}
equipped with an isomorphism
j:\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{M}}\to(-)^{**}
of tensor functors, called a pivotal structure of
{\mathcal{C}}
, is a pivotal fusion category. One can defines a categorical trace
\operatorname{tr}(f)
for any endomorphism
f:x\to x
in a pivotal fusion category. The pivotal structure of a pivotal fusion category is called spherical if
\operatorname{tr}(f)=\operatorname{tr}(f^{*})
for all endomorphisms
f
in
{\mathcal{M}}
. In this case, the pivotal fusion category will be called a spherical fusion category. For any object
x
of a spherical fusion category, we denote by
d^{\mathcal{M}}\!(x):=\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{id}_{x})
, and is called the (pivotal) dimension of
x
. We often drop the superscript when there is not any ambiguity.

A pivotal tensor category
{\mathcal{M}}
is called
strict
if
{\mathcal{M}}
is a strict tensor category and
(-)^{**}=\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{C}}
. Since every pivotal tensor category is equivalent to a strict one (cf.
(
NSind
, Thm. 2.2)
), we may always assume a pivotal fusion category to be strict, and graphically calculus can be applied in this case.

For any objects
x,y
of a spherical fusion category
{\mathcal{M}}
, there is a canonical nondegenerate bilinear form
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:{\mathcal{M}}(x,y){\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}(y,x)\to\mathbb{C}
given by
\langle f,g\rangle=\operatorname{tr}(fg)
for
f\in{\mathcal{M}}(x,y)
and
g\in{\mathcal{M}}(y,x)
.

Similarly, a fusion category equipped with a
braiding
is called a braided fusion category. A
premodular
category
{\mathcal{M}}
is a spherical braided fusion category , and it is often called a
ribbon category
. The underlying ribbon structure
\theta_{x}:x\to x
is a natural isomorphism of the identity functor
\operatorname{id}_{\mathcal{M}}
and it can be constructed from the Drinfeld isomorphism and the spherical pivotal structure of
{\mathcal{M}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{M}}
be a premodular category equipped with the braiding
R
. The
S
-matrix of
{\mathcal{M}}
is a complex square matrix indexed by
\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})
and given by

S^{\mathcal{M}}_{x,y}=\operatorname{tr}(R_{y,x^{*}}R_{x^{*},y})\,.

The premodular category
{\mathcal{M}}
is said to be a
modular tensor category
(MTC) if its
S
-matrix is invertible. For
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})
, the component
\theta^{\mathcal{M}}_{x}
of the ribbon structure
\theta^{\mathcal{M}}
of
{\mathcal{M}}
is a scalar multiple of the identity. In the case we simply identify
\theta_{x}^{\mathcal{M}}
as the scalar and so the
T
-matrix of
{\mathcal{M}}
can written as
T^{\mathcal{M}}_{x,y}=\delta_{x,y}\theta_{x}^{\mathcal{M}}
.

A
pseudounitry
fusion category admits a canonical spherical pivotal structure so that the dimension of any nonzero object is positive (cf.
ENO
). Since there could be choices of pivotal structures on an MTC (cf.
(
RankFinite
, Lem. 2.4)
), we will assume the canonical spherical structure for any pseudounitary MTC or fusion category in this paper.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a modular tensor category with the underlying spherical fusion category being strict. An object
A
of
{\mathcal{C}}
is said to be an algebra if there are morphisms
u_{A}:{\mathds{1}}\to A
and
m_{A}:A{\otimes}A\to A
such that the following diagrams commute:

60.41673pt\raise-15.16666pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{m_{A}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.41673pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-14.38892pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A{\otimes}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 24.63945pt\raise-26.3236pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00417pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{m_{A}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 53.66673pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 53.66673pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

The algebra
A
in
{\mathcal{C}}
is said to be
connected
if
[{\mathds{1}},A]_{\mathcal{C}}=1
, and
commutative
if
m_{A}R_{A,A}=m_{A}
. We borrow the terminology in topological order that an algebra
A
is said to be
condensable
if
A
is commutative and connected such that
d(A)\neq 0
,
\theta_{A}=\operatorname{id}_{A}
and the composition
\varepsilon m_{A}:A{\otimes}A\to{\mathds{1}}
is nondegenerate (cf.
KO02
), where
\varepsilon\in{\mathcal{C}}(A,{\mathds{1}})
denotes the section of
u_{A}
. These conditions imply
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is separable by
(
KO02
, Thm. 3.3)
.

According to
KO02
, if
A
is a condensable algebra in an MTC
{\mathcal{C}}
, the category
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
of right
A
-modules in
{\mathcal{C}}
is also a spherical fusion category with
A
being its the unit object, and tensor product
{\otimes}_{A}
. The pivotal dimension of any object
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
will be denoted by
d_{A}(X)
, which is equal to
\frac{d(X)}{d(A)}
, where
d
is the dimension function of
{\mathcal{C}}

2.2.
A
-modules and their graphical conventions

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an MTC and
A\in{\mathcal{C}}
a condensable algebra. Without loss of generality, we may assume
{\mathcal{C}}
is strict pivotal. The induction function
{\alpha}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
defined
{\alpha}(x)=x{\otimes}A
for
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
and the forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}\to{\mathcal{C}}
are 2-sided adjoints of each other
(
KO02
, Lem. 1.16)
. In particular, we have the natural isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-linear spaces

{\mathcal{C}}_{A}({\alpha}(x),Y)\cong{\mathcal{C}}(x,Y)

and hence
[{\alpha}(x),Y]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=[x,Y]_{{\mathcal{C}}}
for any objects
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
. Since
A
is the unit object of
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
via the multiplication of
A
, we will write
A
as
{\mathds{1}}
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
and denote
\varphi_{x}
the natural isomorphism of vector spaces

(2.1)

\varphi_{x}:{\mathcal{C}}_{A}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})\xrightarrow{\sim}{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)\,,\quad f\mapsto f\circ(x{\otimes}u_{A})

for
f\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
, where
u_{A}:{\mathds{1}}\to A
is the unit map of the algebra
A
. We use the following diagrams for the unit map and multiplication of
A
:

u_{A}:=\,\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.5pt\raise 13.20007pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\circ}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\qquad\text{and}\qquad m_{A}:=\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 24.0pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad,

where the dotted line will always denote the object
A
. Thus, in terms of diagram,

(2.2)

\varphi_{x}(f)\,=\,\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 11.26736pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-10.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 7.65796pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle f}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.26736pt\raise 24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle x}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 3.5pt\raise 24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\circ}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad\text{ for }f\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})\,.

The inverse of
\varphi_{x}
, denoted by
\psi_{x}
, is given by the diagram:

(2.3)

\psi_{x}(g)\,=\,\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 5.26736pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.92378pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle g}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.26736pt\raise 24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle x}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad\text{ for }g\in{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)\,.

For
Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
, we will denote by
\mu_{Y}:Y{\otimes}A\to Y
its underlying
A
-module structure morphism. The right
A
-module
Y
naturally admits left
A
-module structures via the braiding
R
of
{\mathcal{C}}
in two different ways. We abbreviate by
Y^{+}
the
A
-bimodule with the left
A
-module structure
\mu_{Y}^{+}
of
Y
defined by

A{\otimes}Y\xrightarrow{R_{Y,A}^{-1}}Y{\otimes}A\xrightarrow{\mu_{Y}}Y\,.

It is immediate to check that
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(X,Y)={}_{A}{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(X^{+},Y^{+})
and so
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
can be considered as a subcategory of
{}_{A}{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
via the functor
Y\mapsto Y^{+}
.
Similarly,
Y^{-}
is the
A
-bimodule with the left
A
-module structure morphism
\mu_{Y}^{-}
of
Y
defined as

A{\otimes}Y\xrightarrow{R_{A,Y}}Y{\otimes}A\xrightarrow{\mu_{Y}}Y\,.

Unless stated otherwise, every object
Y
of
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
will be considered as the
A
-bimodule
Y^{+}
.

R_{x,y}:x{\otimes}y\to y{\otimes}x

The tensor product
X{\otimes}_{A}Y
for any
X,Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
is defined as the cokernel
X{\otimes}Y\xrightarrow{p_{A}}X{\otimes}_{A}Y
of

X{\otimes}A{\otimes}Y\xrightarrow{\mu_{X}{\otimes}Y-X{\otimes}\mu^{+}_{Y}}X{\otimes}Y\,.

In particular, there are natural isomorphisms of right
A
-modules
l_{Y}:A{\otimes}_{A}Y\to Y
and

which satisfy the commutative diagrams:

The coevaluation

For any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
, both
x{\otimes}Y
and
Y{\otimes}x
are naturally right
A
-module with the structure morphisms

\mu_{x{\otimes}Y}=x{\otimes}\mu_{Y}\quad\text{ and }\quad\mu_{Y{\otimes}x}=(\mu_{Y}{\otimes}x)(Y{\otimes}R_{x,A}),

and
R_{x,Y}:x{\otimes}Y\to Y{\otimes}x
is right
A
-module isomorphism. In particular,
x{\otimes}Y\cong Y{\otimes}x
in
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
. Moreover,
{\alpha}(x)
admits a natural half-braiding
\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),-}
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
given by

\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),Y}:=\left({\alpha}(x){\otimes}_{A}Y\xrightarrow{x{\otimes}l_{Y}}x{\otimes}Y\xrightarrow{R_{x,Y}}Y{\otimes}x\xrightarrow{r_{Y}^{-1}{\otimes}x}Y{\otimes}_{A}{\alpha}(x)\right)\,.

Thus,
({\alpha}(x),\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),-})
is an object in the center
Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
, and we will denote this object in
Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
by
{\alpha}^{+}(x)
as it depends on the “+” choice of the embedding of
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
into
{{}_{A}}{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
. Moreover,
{\alpha}^{+}:{\mathcal{C}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
defines a fully faithful braided tensor functor (cf.
(
DMNO
, Cor. 3.28)
). Since
d_{A}({\alpha}(x))=d(x)
for
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
{\alpha}^{+}
also preserves pivotal structures. Thus,
{\alpha}^{+}({\mathcal{C}})
is a modular subcategory of
Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
. Hence,
{\mathcal{C}}
can be considered as modular subcategory of
Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
via
{\alpha}^{+}
. The half-braiding
\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),Y}
will be simply depicted as

\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),Y}=\vbox{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 10.99176pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-10.99176pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle{\alpha}(x)}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}}}}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 8.18831pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle Y}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}}}{\hbox{\kern 7.19989pt\raise-12.89981pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{}{{{}}{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{{}{}{}{}{{{}}{{{}}{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{}{}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}}\,.

Note that the half-braiding
\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),Y}
is only natural in
Y
and the blue lines or morphisms indicate they are considered in
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
. Moreover,
{\alpha}^{+}({\mathds{1}})=({\mathds{1}},\sigma_{{\alpha}({\mathds{1}}),-})
in
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
, where
\sigma_{{\alpha}({\mathds{1}}),Y}=\operatorname{id}_{Y}
for
Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
.

Recall that the category
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
of local
A
-modules, which are those
Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
satisfying the condition
\mu^{+}_{Y}=\mu_{Y}^{-}
, is a fusion subcategory of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
. Moreover, the braiding of
{\mathcal{C}}
descends to nondegenerate braiding on
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
which makes
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
a modular tensor category (
(
KO02
, Thm. 4.5)
). For
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
,
X
also admits a natural half-braiding
\sigma_{X,-}
on
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
defined as follows: One can check directly

0=\Big(X{\otimes}A{\otimes}Y\xrightarrow{\mu_{X}{\otimes}Y-X{\otimes}\mu^{+}_{Y}}X{\otimes}Y\xrightarrow{R_{Y,X}^{-1}}Y{\otimes}X\xrightarrow{p_{A}}Y{\otimes}_{A}X\Big)\,.

There exists a unique isomorphism
\sigma_{X,Y}:X{\otimes}_{A}Y\to Y{\otimes}_{A}X
of
A
-modules such that the diagram

(2.4)

\textstyle{X{\otimes}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R_{Y,X}^{-1}}
\scriptstyle{p_{A}}
\textstyle{Y{\otimes}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{A}}
\textstyle{X{\otimes}_{A}Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma_{X,Y}}
\textstyle{Y{\otimes}_{A}X}

commutes, and
(X,\sigma_{X,-})\in Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
. It is easy to see that
(X,\sigma_{X,-})
and
{\alpha}^{+}(x)
centralize each other for any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
, and
\beta:({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
,
X\mapsto(X,\sigma_{X,-})
, can be extended to a fully faithful braided tensor functor that preserves dimensions. By comparing the Frobenius-Perron dimensions,
Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})\simeq{\mathcal{C}}\boxtimes({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}
(cf.
(
DMNO
, Cor. 3.30)
). More precisely, the functor

(2.5)

{\alpha}^{+}\boxtimes\beta:{\mathcal{C}}\boxtimes({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}\to Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A}),\,x\boxtimes X\mapsto{\alpha}^{+}(x){\otimes}\beta(X)

defines a braided tensor equivalence preserving pivotal structures.

3.
Fusion actions and Frobenius-Schur indicators

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an MTC and
A
a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
. In this section, we define the actions of the objects of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on
A
, and discuss their relations to generalized Frobenius-Schur indicators, which is essential to the proof of categorical Schur-Weyl duality.

3.1.
Actions of spherical fusion categories on some hom-spaces

In this subsection, we define an action of a spherical fusion category
{\mathcal{B}}
on the hom-spaces
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
for any
\mathbf{X}=(X,\sigma_{X,-})\in Z({\mathcal{B}})
by using some operators whose traces are generalized Frobenius-Schur indicators.

The notion of the 2nd Frobenius-Schur indicators was introduced for the complex representations of finite groups. They have been generalized to the settings of semisimple Hopf algebras
LM
, quasi-Hopf algebras
MNg
;
NS4
, rational conformal field theory
Ban
and fusion categories
FGSV
;
FS
. The higher Frobenius-Schur indicator
\nu_{n}(V)
for any representation
V
of a semisimple Hopf algebra was introduced in
LM
, and studied extensively in
KSZ
. These developments inspired further generalizations in semisimple Hopf algebras
SZ
, and in pivotal tensor categories
NS07
;
NSind
;
NS10
. The fusion actions to be defined involve the generalized Frobenius-Schur indicators for spherical fusion categories introduced in
NS10
, which are briefly recalled as follows.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a spherical fusion category. For any pair
(n,k)
of integers,
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}
and
\mathbf{X}=(X,\sigma_{X,-})\in Z({\mathcal{B}})
. Assuming the conventions
Y^{{\otimes}n}=(Y^{*})^{{\otimes}|n|}
if
n<0
and
Y^{{\otimes}0}={\mathds{1}}
in
{\mathcal{B}}
, one can define a linear operator
E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(n,k)}:{\mathcal{B}}(X,Y^{{\otimes}n{}})\to{\mathcal{B}}(X,Y^{{\otimes}n})
via the half-braiding
\sigma_{X,-}
of
\mathbf{X}
. The generalized Frobenius-Schur indicator is defined as
\nu_{(n,k)}^{\mathbf{X}}(Y):=\operatorname{Tr}(E^{(n,k)}_{\mathbf{X},Y})
. Here
\operatorname{Tr}
denotes the ordinary trace of a linear operator. These generalized Frobenius-Schur indicators are related by the
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-actions on
K(Z({\mathcal{B}}))
and on
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. These relations will be discussed in more detail when we prove the Schur-Weyl duality in the next section.

For
n>0
, the operator
E^{(n,1)}_{\mathbf{X},Y}
is invertible and
E^{(n,k)}_{\mathbf{X},Y}=\Big(E^{(n,1)}_{\mathbf{X},Y}\Big)^{k}
. In particular,
E^{(n,0)}_{\mathbf{X},Y}=\operatorname{id}
and hence

(3.6)

\nu_{(n,0)}^{\mathbf{X}}(Y)=[X,Y^{{\otimes}n}]_{\mathcal{B}}\,.

If
{\mathcal{B}}
is strict pivotal, the operator
E^{(n,1)}_{\mathbf{X},Y}
can be defined diagrammatically, namely

E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(n,1)}:{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 11.37184pt\hbox{{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-10.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

The operator
E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,-1)}
defines a left action of
Y
on
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
. Recall from
(
NS10
, Sec. 2)
that
E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,-1)}:{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})\to{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
is a
\mathbb{C}
-linear operator defined by

\displaystyle E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,-1)}(f)

\displaystyle:=\Big(X\xrightarrow{r^{-1}}X{\otimes}{\mathds{1}}\xrightarrow{X{\otimes}\operatorname{coev}}X{\otimes}(Y{\otimes}Y^{*})\xrightarrow{a^{-1}_{X,Y,Y^{*}}}(X{\otimes}Y){\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{\sigma_{X,Y}{\otimes}Y^{*}}

\displaystyle(Y{\otimes}X){\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{(Y{\otimes}f){\otimes}Y^{*}}(Y{\otimes}{\mathds{1}}){\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{r{\otimes}Y}Y{\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{j{\otimes}V^{*}}Y^{**}{\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{ev}}A\Big),

where
a
and
r
respectively denote the associativity and the right unit isomorphisms of
{\mathcal{B}}
. If
{\mathcal{B}}
is strict pivotal, we can describe
E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,-1)}
as

(3.7)

E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,-1)}:\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 6.55106pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.65796pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle f}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.55106pt\raise 24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle X}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}}\quad\mapsto\quad\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 27.81151pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.65796pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle f}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.55106pt\raise 26.39996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle X}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}}}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}}}{\hbox{\kern 7.19989pt\raise-12.89981pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{}{{{}}{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{{}{}{}{}{{{}}{{{}}{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{}{}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}}.

This leads us to the following definition of
K({\mathcal{B}})
-action on
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a spherical fusion category.
For
\mathbf{X}=(X,\sigma_{X,-})\in Z({\mathcal{B}})
and
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we define the (left) action of
Y
on
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
as

Y\cdot f:=E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,-1)}(f)\quad\text{for }f\in{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})\,.

In particular,
\operatorname{Tr}(Y,V_{\mathbf{X}})=\nu_{(0,-1)}^{\mathbf{X}}(Y)
by definition, where
V_{\mathbf{X}}={\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
. This action of objects in
{\mathcal{B}}
on
V_{\mathbf{X}}
can be extended
\mathbb{Z}
-linearly to an action of
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
, and we will simply call this
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
-action on
V_{\mathbf{X}}
as
the fusion action of

{\mathcal{B}}
.

Remark 3.2
.

It follows from the proof of
(
NS10
, Prop. 2.8)
that
E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,k)}=E_{\mathbf{X},Y^{{\otimes}(-k)}}^{(0,-1)}
for any integer
k
. In particular,
Y^{*}\cdot f=E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,1)}(f)
, which defines a right action of
Y
on
{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbf{X},{\mathds{1}})
by the following proposition.

We first show that
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
is a left
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
-module with such fusion action of
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a spherical fusion category.
The fusion action of
{\mathcal{B}}
on
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
introduced in
3.1
defines an
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
-module. In particular, its character value at
Y
is the generalized Frobenius-Schur indicator
\nu_{(0,-1)}^{\mathbf{X}}(Y)
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume
{\mathcal{B}}
is strict pivotal. For any
\mathbf{X}\in Z({\mathcal{B}})
and
f\in{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
, we obviously have
{\mathds{1}}\cdot f=f
. For any objects
V,W\in{\mathcal{B}}
,

(V{\otimes}W)\cdot f=\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 33.82227pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.65796pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle f}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.55106pt\raise 36.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle X}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}}}}}}}}}}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}{{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-33.82227pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle V}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}}}}}}}}}}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}{{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-12.09854pt\raise-10.79993pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle W}$}}}}}}}}}}\qquad=V\cdot(W\cdot f)\,.

The last assertion follows directly from the definition of generalized Frobenius-Schur indicators.
∎

Now, for any fusion subcategory
\mathcal{D}
of
{\mathcal{B}}
and
\mathbf{X}=(X,\sigma_{X,-})\in Z({\mathcal{B}})
,
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
is then an
K({\mathcal{B}})
-module by Proposition
3.3
, and we define

(3.8)

{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})^{\mathcal{D}}:=\{f\in{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})\mid Yf=d(Y)f\text{ for all }Y\in{\mathcal{D}}\}

which is called the
{\mathcal{D}}
-
invariant subspace
of
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
.

The hom-space
Z({\mathcal{B}})(\mathbf{X},{\mathds{1}})
can be characterized as the
{\mathcal{B}}
-invariant subspace of
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
in the following lemma.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be a spherical fusion category.
For
\mathbf{X}=(X,\sigma_{X,-})\in Z({\mathcal{B}})
,

Z({\mathcal{B}})(\mathbf{X},{\mathds{1}})={\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})^{{\mathcal{B}}}\,.

Proof.

For
f\in Z({\mathcal{B}})(\mathbf{X},{\mathds{1}})
and
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we have

Y\cdot f={\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 27.81151pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.65796pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle f}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-24.55106pt\raise 26.39996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle X}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle Y}$}}}}}}}}}}}\qquad=d(Y)f\,.

Therefore,
Z({\mathcal{B}})(\mathbf{X},{\mathds{1}})\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})^{{\mathcal{B}}}
.
Conversely, if
f\in{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})^{{\mathcal{B}}}
, then
Yf=d(Y)f
for all
Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{B}})
. Consider the nondegenerate bilinear form
\langle g,Yf\rangle=d(Y)\langle g,f\rangle
for
g\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}},X)
. This means

or

∎

3.2.
Fusion actions on condensable subalgebras

Now, we consider a condensable algebra
A
in any modular tensor category
{\mathcal{C}}
. Since
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is a spherical fusion category and
{\alpha}^{+}(x)\in Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
for any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
, we have the fusion action of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
by the preceding subsection. We will define the fusion action of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on the algebra
A
in Definition
3.8
.

The notion of the objects
Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
acting on
A
is equivalent to an algebra homomorphism
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\to{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
. Since
{\mathcal{C}}
is semisimple, compositions of morphisms provide the decomposition

{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)=\bigoplus_{x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}{\mathcal{C}}(x,A){\otimes}{\mathcal{C}}(A,x)\,.

We proceed to define an action of
K_{0}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
of
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
for
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
. The adjunction (
2.1
) insinuates an action of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
on
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
which is equivalent the fusion action of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
.

Recall that the evaluation
\overline{\operatorname{ev}}_{Y}:Y^{*}{\otimes}_{A}Y\to A
and coevaluation
\overline{\operatorname{coev}}_{Y}:A\to Y{\otimes}_{A}Y^{*}
morphisms for the left dual of
Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
are determined by the morphisms
\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{Y}:Y^{*}{\otimes}Y\to A
and
\widetilde{\operatorname{coev}}_{Y}:A\to Y{\otimes}Y^{*}
in
{\mathcal{C}}
given by the diagrams

The adjunction (
2.1
) and Definition
3.1
inspire the following definition of fusion action of
K_{0}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
on
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
for any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
.

Definition 3.5
.

For
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
and
g\in{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
, we define:

\displaystyle Y\cdot g=

\displaystyle\Big(x\xrightarrow{x{\otimes}\operatorname{coev}_{Y}}x{\otimes}Y{\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{R_{x,Y}{\otimes}Y^{*}}Y{\otimes}x{\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{Y{\otimes}g{\otimes}Y^{*}}Y{\otimes}A{\otimes}Y^{*}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mu_{Y}{\otimes}Y^{*}}Y{\otimes}Y^{*}=Y^{**}{\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{\operatorname{ev}}_{Y^{*}}}A\Big)\,.

In terms of diagram with the omission of “
\cdot
”, we have

(3.9)

For
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
{\alpha}^{+}(x)=({\alpha}(x),\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),-})\in Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
where the half-braiding
\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),Y}
is given by

\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),Y}:=\left(x{\otimes}A{\otimes}_{A}Y\xrightarrow{x{\otimes}l_{Y}^{-1}}x{\otimes}Y\xrightarrow{R_{x,Y}}Y{\otimes}x\xrightarrow{\sim}Y{\otimes}_{A}x{\otimes}A\right)

for
Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
. Now, we can prove
\varphi_{x}
is an isomorphism of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-modules.

Proposition 3.6
.

The natural isomorphism
\varphi_{x}:{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})\to{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
, for any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
, is an intertwining operator of
K_{0}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-actions on
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
and
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
given respectively by Definitions
3.1
and
3.5
. In particular,
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
is an
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-module.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we may assume both
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
are strict pivotal. For
Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
and
f\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
,

\displaystyle\varphi_{x}(Yf)=\Big(x\xrightarrow{x{\otimes}u_{A}}{\alpha}(x)={\alpha}(x){\otimes\!_{A}}A\xrightarrow{x{\otimes}\overline{\operatorname{coev}}}{\alpha}(x){\otimes\!_{A}}Y{\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{\sigma_{{\alpha}(x),Y}{\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}}

\displaystyle Y{\otimes\!_{A}}{\alpha}(x){\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{Y{\otimes\!_{A}}f{\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}}Y{\otimes\!_{A}}A{\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}=Y^{**}{\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{\overline{\operatorname{ev}}}A\Big)\,.

The commutativity of the following diagram

\textstyle{{\alpha}(x)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{a(x){\otimes\!_{A}}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x{\otimes}\overline{\operatorname{coev}}}
\textstyle{{\alpha}(x){\otimes\!_{A}}Y{\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma{\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}}
\textstyle{Y{\otimes\!_{A}}{\alpha}(x){\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}}
\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{x{\otimes}u_{A}}
\scriptstyle{x{\otimes}\operatorname{coev}_{Y}}
\textstyle{x{\otimes}Y{\otimes}Y^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{A}}
\scriptstyle{R_{x,Y}{\otimes}Y^{*}}
\textstyle{Y{\otimes}x{\otimes}Y^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{A}}

\scriptstyle{Y{\otimes\!_{A}}f{\otimes\!_{A}}Y^{*}}

\textstyle{A}

implies
\varphi_{x}(Yf)
equals to the composition

\displaystyle x

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{x{\otimes}\operatorname{coev}}x{\otimes}Y{\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{R_{x,Y}{\otimes}Y^{*}}Y{\otimes}x{\otimes}Y^{*}\xrightarrow{Y{\otimes}\varphi_{x}(f){\otimes}Y^{*}}Y{\otimes}A{\otimes}Y^{*}

which is
Y\varphi_{x}(f)
by Definition
3.5
.
∎

Similarly, for any fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
and
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
, we define

(3.10)

{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)^{\mathcal{B}}=\{g\in{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)\mid Yg=d_{A}(Y)g\text{ for all }Y\in{\mathcal{B}}\}

which is the isotypic component of
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
corresponding to the dimension character of
K({\mathcal{B}})
, and is called the
{\mathcal{B}}
-
invariant subspace
of
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
. The fusion subcategory
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
can be characterized as follows:

Proposition 3.7
.

If
Y
is a simple
A
-module but
Y\not\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
, then
\operatorname{tr}(Y\operatorname{id}_{A})=0
, where
\operatorname{tr}
is the categorical trace of
{\mathcal{C}}
. Moreover, a fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is contained in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
if and only if

(3.11)

{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)^{\mathcal{B}}={\mathcal{C}}(x,A)\quad\text{ for all }x\in{\mathcal{C}}\,.

In particular,
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
is the fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
satisfying (
3.11
) with the greatest Frobenius-Perron dimension.

and so
Yg=d_{A}(Y)g
. Thus,
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)^{{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}}={\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
for all
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
, and so any fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
satisfies
(
3.11
)
.

On the other hand, if
Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}
but
Y\not\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
, we consider the action of
Y
on
\operatorname{id}_{A}\in{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
. Then

where the last equality follows from
(
KO02
, Lem. 4.3)
, and so
Y\operatorname{id}_{A}\neq d_{A}(Y)\operatorname{id}_{A}
.
In particular, if
{\mathcal{B}}
is not a fusion subcategory of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
, then
{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)^{\mathcal{B}}\neq{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
.
∎

Now, we define the action of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on the algebra
A
.

Definition 3.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be any MTC and
A\in{\mathcal{C}}
a condensable algebra. We define the
\mathbb{C}
-linear map
\rho:K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\to{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
by

\rho(Y)=Y\operatorname{id}_{A}=\frac{1}{d(A)}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 21.3313pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 16.19989pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle Y}$}}}}}}}}}}\quad\text{for }Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\,.

The next lemma proves that
\rho
defines an action of
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
on
A
, and so
\rho
will be referred as the fusion action of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on
A
. We would like to highlight that an analog of Lemma
3.9
was established in Theorem 5 and in the proof Theorem 6 of
Rie2025
.

Lemma 3.9
.

The linear map
\rho
is a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra homomorphism that preserves duality, i.e.
\rho(Y^{*})=\rho(Y)^{*}
for all
Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that
\rho
respects the multiplication of simple objects for the first assertion. For any
X,Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}
, we have

\displaystyle\rho(X)\circ\rho(Y)

Here the second last equation follows from
(
KO02
, Lem. 1.21)
.

We proceed to prove the equality
\rho(Y^{*})=\rho(Y)^{*}
by using the nondegeneracy of the pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)\times{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)\to\mathbb{C}
. For any
f\in{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
,

f,\rho(Y^{*})\rangle\,,

where the fifth equality follows from the spherical pivotal structure, and the 7-th equality is a consequence of
\theta_{A}=\operatorname{id}_{A}
and
A
being commutative. The nondegeneracy of
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
and
d(A)\neq 0
imply
\rho(Y^{*})=\rho(Y)^{*}
.
∎

With some explanations, the fact that
\rho
is an algebra homomorphism can also be obtained from Proposition
3.6
.

4.
Schur-Weyl duality

We continue our development of the fusion action of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
on the condensable algebra
A
in a modular tensor category
{\mathcal{C}}
, and prove the categorical Schur-Weyl duality in Theorem
4.1
.

Recall that for any MTC
{\mathcal{M}}
, the irreducible characters
\chi_{X}
of
K({\mathcal{M}})
are 1-dimensional and parametrized by
X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})
. More precisely,
\chi_{X}(Y)=\frac{S^{\mathcal{M}}_{X,Y}}{d^{\mathcal{M}}(X)}
for all
Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})
, where
d^{\mathcal{M}}(X)
denotes the pivotal dimension of
X
in
{\mathcal{M}}
and
S^{\mathcal{M}}
is the (unnormalized)
S
-matrix of
{\mathcal{M}}
. In particular,
\chi_{\mathds{1}}(Y)=d^{\mathcal{M}}(Y)
. Thus, the indecomposable central idempotent
e^{\mathcal{M}}_{X}
of
K({\mathcal{M}})
are also indexed by
X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})
so that

u\,e^{\mathcal{M}}_{X}=\chi_{X}(u)\,e^{\mathcal{M}}_{X}
for
u\in K({\mathcal{M}})
. These idempotents can be expressed in terms of the
S
-matrix of
{\mathcal{M}}
, namely

e^{\mathcal{M}}_{X}=\frac{d^{\mathcal{M}}(X)}{\dim({\mathcal{M}})}\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})}S^{\mathcal{M}}_{X,Y}Y^{*}\,.

One can directly verify by using the Verlinde formula that
\{e_{X}^{\mathcal{M}}\mid X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}})\}
is the set primitive idempotents of
K({\mathcal{M}})
.

If
{\mathcal{B}}
is a spherical fusion category, then
{\mathcal{M}}=Z({\mathcal{B}})
is an MTC
MugerSF2
. It follows from
ENO
that the forgetful functor
F:{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{B}}
induces an algebra homomorphism
{\underline{F}}:K({\mathcal{M}})\to K({\mathcal{B}})
, and its image is the center of
K({\mathcal{B}})
. By
(
O15
, Thm. 2.13)
,
e^{\mathcal{M}}_{X}\in\ker{\underline{F}}
if and only if
[F(X):{\mathds{1}}]_{\mathcal{B}}=0
.

Now, we return to our setting with
{\mathcal{B}}={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
. By
DMNO
or (
2.5
),
Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
is equivalent to
{\mathcal{C}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{A}}
as spherical braided fusion categories, where
{\mathcal{A}}=({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}
. Thus,

\operatorname{Irr}(Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A}))=\{{\alpha}^{+}(x){\otimes}_{A}Y\mid x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}),Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})\}\,.

and the set of indecomposable central idempotents of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
is given by

(4.12)

\{e_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}}e_{Y}^{{\mathcal{A}}}\mid x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\text{ and }Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})\text{ such that }[{\alpha}(x){\otimes}_{A}Y,{\mathds{1}}]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}\neq 0\}.

Now, we can explicitly state and prove our Theorem I as follows.

Theorem 4.1

(Schur-Weyl duality)
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be an MTC and
A\in{\mathcal{C}}
a condensable algebra.
Let
{\mathcal{A}}=({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}
and
\rho:K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\to{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
the
\mathbb{C}
-algebra homomorphism defined in Definition
3.8
. Then

(i)

\ker\rho
is the ideal of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
generated by
1-e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}.

(ii)

{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
is an irreducible
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-module for any
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
with
[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}\neq 0.

(iii)

For any
x,y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
,
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)\cong{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)
as
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-modules if and only if
x=y.

(iv)

The restriction
\rho:e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\to{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-algebras.

Proof.

(i) Note that
e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}:=\frac{1}{\dim({\mathcal{A}})}\sum_{X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})}d_{A}(X)X
is an indecomposable idempotent of
K({\mathcal{A}})
corresponding the character
\chi_{\mathds{1}}(X)=d_{A}(X)
for
X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})
.
By Proposition
3.7
,
\rho(X)=d_{A}(X)\operatorname{id}_{A}
for
X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})
, and so we find
\rho(e^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathds{1}})=\operatorname{id}_{A}
. Thus
1-e^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathds{1}}\in\ker\rho
and so the ideal of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
generated by
1-e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}
is contained in
\ker\rho
.
Since
e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}
is a central element of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
,
(1-e^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathds{1}})K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
and

e^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathds{1}}K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
are two sided ideal and
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})=(1-e^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathds{1}})K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\oplus e^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathds{1}}K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}).
For convenience we set
\overline{K}=e^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathds{1}}K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}).

From (
4.12
) on the indecomposable central idempotents of

K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
, we see that the indecomposable central idempotents
\overline{K}
are given by

(4.13)

\{e^{\mathcal{A}}_{\mathds{1}}e_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}}\mid x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\text{ such that }[{\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}}]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}\neq 0\}.

To complete the proof of this statement, it suffices to show that
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}e_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}})\neq 0
for
[{\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}}]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}\neq 0
or
[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}\neq 0
.

Let
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
such that
[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}\neq 0
. For any
y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
and
f\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
,

(4.14)

{\alpha}(y)f=\hskip-5.0pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 34.87776pt\hbox{\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-5.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 2.65796pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle f}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.99176pt\raise 26.39996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle{\alpha}(x)}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}}}}}}}}}}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}{{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-34.87776pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern f}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-28.99176pt\raise 26.39996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern
