0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}}}}}}}}}}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}{{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-36.07784pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\scriptstyle{\alpha}(y)}$}}}}}}}}}}}\hskip 20.0pt=\,\frac{S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x^{*},y}}{d(x)}f\,.

Therefore, for
z\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
,

e_{z}^{{\mathcal{C}}}f=\frac{d(z)}{\dim({\mathcal{C}})}\sum_{y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}S^{\mathcal{C}}_{z,y}{\alpha}(y^{*})f=\frac{d(z)}{\dim({\mathcal{C}})}\sum_{y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}\frac{S^{\mathcal{C}}_{z,y}S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x^{*},y^{*}}}{d(x)}f=\delta_{z,x^{*}}f\,.

Thus,
e_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
acts as identity on
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x^{*}),{\mathds{1}})
or
{\mathcal{C}}(x^{*},A)
by Proposition
3.6
.

For any
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
, let
B^{x}=\{\iota_{x,i}\}_{i}
be a basis for
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
, and
B_{x}=\{\pi_{x,i}\}_{i}
the dual basis of
B^{x}
for
{\mathcal{C}}(A,x)
relative to the nondegenerate bilinear form
{\mathcal{C}}(A,x)\times{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)\to{\mathcal{C}}(x,x)
defined by composition, i.e.
\pi_{x,i}\circ\iota_{x,j}=\delta_{i,j}\operatorname{id}_{x}
. Then
P_{x}=\sum_{i}\iota_{x,i}\circ\pi_{x,i}\in{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
is an idempotent, and the image of
P_{x}
is the isotypic component of
x
in
A
. Moreover,
\operatorname{id}_{A}=\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}P_{x}
. In particular,

\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}e_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}})=\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}})\circ\rho(e_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}})=e_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}}\operatorname{id}_{A}=P_{x^{*}}\neq 0

for all
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
. Thus, the restriction
\rho|_{\overline{K}}
is injective or
\ker\rho=(1-e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}})K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
.

(ii) Let
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
. It follows from (i) that
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
is a (complex) representation of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
such that all but one indecomposable central idempotents of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
acts trivially on
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
and
e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}e_{x^{*}}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
acts as identity. Let
M_{x}
be a simple
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-module such that
e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}e_{x^{*}}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
acts as identity of
M_{x}
. Then
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})\cong n_{x}M_{x}
for some positive integer
n_{x}
, and

{\alpha}(y)f=\frac{S_{x^{*},y}}{d(x)}f
for
y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}),f\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
.

By
Lu03
, the element

\phi_{M_{x}}=\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\operatorname{Tr}(Y,M_{x})Y^{*}\in K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})

is central and it acts as a scalar
f_{M_{x}}
on
M_{x}
and zero on any other irreducible
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-modules. The scalar
f_{M_{x}}
, called the
formal codegree
of
M_{x}
, was proved in
(
O15
, Thm. 2.13)
that

f_{M_{x}}=\frac{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}{d_{A}({\alpha}(x^{*}))}=\frac{\dim({\mathcal{C}})}{d(x)d(A)}\,.

Let
W_{x}
denote the
K_{0}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-module
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),1)
. Then

\phi_{W_{x}}=\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\operatorname{Tr}(Y,W_{x})Y^{*}=n_{x}\phi_{M_{x}}

and it acts on
W_{x}
as the scalar
n_{x}f_{M_{x}}
, and so

(4.15)

\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{W_{x}},W_{x})=n_{x}f_{M_{x}}[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}=n_{x}\frac{\dim({\mathcal{C}})[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}}{d(A)d(x)}\,.

Recall from Section
3
that
\operatorname{Tr}(Y,W_{x})=\operatorname{Tr}(E_{{\alpha}(x),Y}^{(0,-1)})=\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(X)}(Y)
where
E_{{\alpha}(x),Y}^{(0,-1)}
is the endomorphism on
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
defined in terms of diagram (
3.7
). We proceed to compute
\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{W_{x}},W_{x})
by using properties of generalized Frobenius-Schur indicators
\nu_{(n,k)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)
, which was synopsized in Section
3
. The readers are referred to
NS10
for more detail on their arithmetic properties, which are briefly summarized as follows.

The modular group
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, generated by
\mathfrak{s}={\begin{bmatrix}0&-1\\
1&0\end{bmatrix}}
and
\mathfrak{t}={\begin{bmatrix}1&1\\
0&1\end{bmatrix}}
, admits a canonical action on
K(Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A}))
. Since
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
is equivalent to
{\mathcal{C}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{A}}
as braided spherical fusion categories, the simple objects of
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
are indexed by the pairs
(x,X)\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\times{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})}
. The pair
(x,X)\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\times{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})}
is identified with the simple object
{\alpha}^{+}(x)\boxtimes X
in
Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
(cf. (
2.5
)).

Let
\mathbb{S}
and
\mathbb{T}
be the
S
and
T
matrices of
Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
. They are indexed by the set
\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\times{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})}
, and

\mathbb{S}_{(x,X),(y,Y)}=S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}S^{\mathcal{A}}_{X,Y}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathbb{T}_{(x,X),(y,Y)}=\delta_{X,Y}\delta_{x,y}\theta_{x}^{{\mathcal{C}}}\theta^{\mathcal{A}}_{X}

for any
(x,X),(y,Y)\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\times{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})}
, where
\theta^{\mathcal{C}}_{x}
and
\theta_{X}^{\mathcal{A}}
are the scalars of the components of the ribbon structures
\theta^{\mathcal{C}}
and
\theta^{\mathcal{A}}
.

For simplicity, we will write
{\alpha}^{+}(x)X
for
{\alpha}^{+}(x)\boxtimes X
in
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
and
{\alpha}(x)X
for
{\alpha}(x){\otimes}_{A}X
in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
. Then, the
\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
-action on
K(Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A}))
is given by

(4.16)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{s}\cdot{\alpha}^{+}(x)X

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\\
Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})}\end{subarray}}\frac{S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}S^{\mathcal{A}}_{X,Y}}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\,{\alpha}^{+}(y)Y,

(4.17)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{t}\cdot{\alpha}^{+}(x)X

\displaystyle=\theta_{x}^{\mathcal{C}}\theta_{X}^{\mathcal{A}}\,{\alpha}^{+}(x)X\,.

For any
(n,k)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, the generalized Frobenius-Schur indicator
\nu_{(n,k)}^{\mathbf{X}}(Y)
, defined for any objects
\mathbf{X}
in
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
and
Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
, is additive in
\mathbf{X}
and
Y
. Based on the discussion for (
3.6
),

(4.18)

\nu_{(1,0)}^{\mathbf{X}}(Y)=[X,Y]_{{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}}\quad\text{for }\mathbf{X}=(X,\sigma_{X,-})\in Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\,.

One can extend
\nu_{(n,k)}^{\mathbf{X}}(Y)
linearly to a functional
\nu_{(n,k)}^{z}(Y)
for
z\in K(Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A}))
. By
(
NS10
, Prop. 3.3, Lem. 5.8 (i))
, we have

(4.19)

\nu_{(n,k)}^{\mathfrak{s}^{2}\cdot\mathbf{X}}(Y)=\nu_{(n,k)}^{\mathbf{X}^{*}}(Y)=\nu_{(n,k)}^{\mathbf{X}}(Y^{*})

for
\mathbf{X}\in Z({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
and

(4.20)

\nu_{(n,k)}^{\mathfrak{g}\cdot z}(Y)=\nu_{(n,k)\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}}^{z}(Y)

for any
\mathfrak{g}\in\operatorname{SL}_{2}(\mathbb{Z})
, where
\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}=\mathfrak{j}\mathfrak{g}\mathfrak{j}
with
\mathfrak{j}={\begin{bmatrix}1&0\\
0&-1\end{bmatrix}}
and
(n,k)\tilde{\mathfrak{g}}
denotes the matrix multiplication.

By the definition of
\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)
, we first rewrite

\phi_{W_{x}}=\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)Y^{*}

and so

\displaystyle\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{W_{x}},W_{x})=

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)\operatorname{Tr}(Y^{*},W_{x})=\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)\cdot\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y^{*})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{by }\eqref{eq:dual}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)\cdot\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)^{*}}(Y)=\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)\cdot\nu_{(0,-1)}^{\mathfrak{s}^{2}\cdot{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)\cdot\nu_{(1,0)}^{\mathfrak{s}\cdot{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)\quad\text{by }\eqref{eq:equivariance}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\\
y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}),X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})\end{subarray}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)\cdot\frac{S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}S^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathds{1}},X}}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\nu_{(1,0)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(y)X}(Y)\quad\text{by }\eqref{eq:s-action}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\\
y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}),X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})\end{subarray}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}(Y)\cdot\frac{S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}S^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathds{1}},X}}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}[{\alpha}(y)X,Y]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}\quad\text{by }\eqref{eq:ind(1,0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})\\
y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\end{subarray}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}({\alpha}(y)X)\cdot\frac{S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}S^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathds{1}},X}}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}X\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})\\
y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})\end{subarray}}\nu_{(0,-1)}^{{\alpha}^{+}(x)}({\alpha}(y))\cdot\frac{d_{A}(X)S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}S^{\mathcal{A}}_{{\mathds{1}},X}}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\quad\text{by Proposition }\ref{p:CAo}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}\frac{S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x^{*},y}}{d(x)}[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}\frac{\dim({\mathcal{A}})S^{\mathcal{C}}_{x,y}}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\quad\text{by }\eqref{eq:indicator(a(y))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,\,\frac{\dim({\mathcal{C}})\dim({\mathcal{A}})[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}}{d(x)\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}=\frac{\dim({\mathcal{C}})[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}}{d(x)d(A)}\,.

It follows from (
4.15
) that
n_{x}=1
, and hence
W_{x}
is an irreducible
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-module.

(iii) From the proofs of (i) and (ii), we know that, for any simple object
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
is a simple
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
such that
e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}e_{x^{*}}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
acts as identity on
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
and all other indecomposable central idempotents of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
act trivially on it. It follows immediately that
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x),{\mathds{1}})
and
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(y),{\mathds{1}})
, for any simple objects
x,y\in{\mathcal{C}}
, are inequivalent
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
-modules if
x\not\cong y.

(iv) By (i)-(iii), for
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
with
[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}\neq 0
,
P_{x}
defined in the proof of (i) is an indecomposable central idempotent of
{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
, and the simple ideal of
{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
generated by
P_{x}
has dimension
[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}^{2}
. Since
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}e_{x}^{\mathcal{C}})=P_{x^{*}}
, the assertion (iv)
follows from (
4.13
) on the indecomposable central idempotents
of
\overline{K}
. ∎

The following corollary is a refinement of
(
O15
, Cor. 2.16)
and it also addresses the question thereafter.

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}
be any spherical fusion category,
{\mathcal{C}}=Z({\mathcal{B}})
the center of
{\mathcal{B}}
,
I:{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{C}}
the right adjoint of the forgetful functor from
{\mathcal{C}}
to
{\mathcal{B}}
, and
A=I({\mathds{1}})
. Then

\rho:K({\mathcal{B}})\to{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
defined in
3.8
is an isomorphism of
\mathbb{C}
-algebras. Moreover, for any
\mathbf{X}=(X,\sigma_{X,-})\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
the representation of
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
,
Y\mapsto E_{\mathbf{X},Y}^{(0,-1)}
, on the vector space
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
is irreducible if and only if
\mathbf{X}
is a simple object in
{\mathcal{C}}
and
[X,{\mathds{1}}]_{\mathcal{B}}>0
. Furthermore, every irreducible representation of
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
is of this form, and
{\mathcal{B}}(X,{\mathds{1}})
and
{\mathcal{B}}(Y,{\mathds{1}})
are equivalent
K({\mathcal{B}})
-modules if and only if
\mathbf{X}\cong\mathbf{Y}
in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof.

Since
A
is a Lagrangian algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
,
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}=\operatorname{Vec}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}\simeq{\mathcal{B}}
as spherical fusion categories. Now the result follows immediately from Theorem
4.1
.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

For any condensable algebra
A
in a modular tensor category
{\mathcal{C}}
,
A=\bigoplus_{x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}\,x
as objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
. One can identify
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
as the object
[x,A]_{\mathcal{C}}\,{\mathds{1}}
in
{\mathcal{C}}
and write

A=\bigoplus_{x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}{\mathcal{C}}(x,A){\otimes}x\,.

Since
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)
is an irreducible representation of
\overline{K}=e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}{K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
, the simple subobjects
x
of
A
are parametrized by the irreducible characters
\lambda
of
\overline{K}
and so

A=\bigoplus_{\lambda\in\operatorname{Irr}(\overline{K})}W_{\lambda}{\otimes}x_{\lambda}

where
W_{\lambda}
is an
\overline{K}
-module which affords the character
\lambda
. Thus, Theorem
4.1
is a categorical generalization of Schur-Weyl duality.

5.
Galois correspondence from fusion actions

In this section, we prove the Galois correspondence between the condensable subalgebras
B
of any given condensable algebra
A
in a pseudounitary MTC
{\mathcal{C}}
and the fusion subcategories
{\mathcal{B}}
containing
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
. In particular, we prove that
B=A^{\mathcal{B}}
is a condensable subalgebra of
A
, and
{\mathcal{B}}\mapsto A^{\mathcal{B}}
defines a order-reversing bijection between the fusion subcategory of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
containing
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
and the condensable subalgebra of
A
. Moreover,
{\mathcal{B}}=({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}
. Our approach is motivated by
DM1997
;
Xu2014
, and is a generalization of Galois correspondence
DM1997
;
HMT1999
;
DJX2013
;
Xu2014
of orbifolds of vertex operator algebras, and that of intermediate conformal nets.

We would like to point out that such a correspondence was previously established in
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.1)
for
{\mathcal{C}}=Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
or
A
is the Lagrangian algebra of
{\mathcal{C}}
, where the subalgebra
B
of
A
corresponding to the fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is given by
B=I_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
and
I_{\mathcal{B}}
is the right adjoint of the forgetful functor
F_{\mathcal{B}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
. This approach will be discussed in detail in Section
6
. In this section, we essentially provides an alternate approach for
(
DMNO
, Rmk. 4.12)
in the spirit of classical Galois correspondence. The remark
(
DMNO
, Rmk. 4.12)
was also proved in
(
LKW
, Thm. 3.5)
.

Recall that every morphism
f:x\to y
in an abelian category has a factorization
f=\iota\pi
, in which
\pi:x\to z
is an epimorphism and
\iota:z\to y
is a monomorphism for some object
z
(cf.
(
MacLane
, VIII.3)
). This monic-epi factorization is unique up to isomorphism, which means that if
f=\iota^{\prime}\pi^{\prime}
for some epimorphism
\pi^{\prime}:x\to z^{\prime}
and monomorphism
\iota^{\prime}:z^{\prime}\to y
, there exists an isomorphism
h:z\to z^{\prime}
such that the following diagram commutes:

\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{z\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h}
\scriptstyle{\iota}
\textstyle{y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{x\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}
\textstyle{z^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\prime}}
\textstyle{y\,.}

The subobject
(z,\iota)
of
y
is called the
image
of
f
, and the pair
(\pi,z)
is called its
coimage
.

Let
A
be a condensable algebra in an MTC
{\mathcal{C}}
with the algebra structure
(u_{A},m_{A})
, and
{\mathcal{B}}
a fusion subcategory of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
containing
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}={\mathcal{A}}
. Recall that

e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}}=\frac{1}{\dim({\mathcal{B}})}\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{B}})}}d_{A}(Y)Y

is a primitive central idempotent of
{K({\mathcal{B}})}
, but it may not be central in
K={K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
. Since
{\mathcal{A}}=({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}
is a fusion subcategory of
{\mathcal{B}}
,
e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{A}}}e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}}=e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}}
, and
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
is an idempotent in
{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
, which has a factorization
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})=\iota\pi
, where
\iota:B\to A
the image of
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
and
\pi:A\to B
its coimage. The subobject
\iota:B\to A
of
A
will be denoted by
(B,\iota)
. It is immediate to see that
B\cong\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)^{{\mathcal{B}}}{\otimes}x
as objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
, where
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)^{{\mathcal{B}}}
is identified with
\dim({\mathcal{C}}(x,A)^{{\mathcal{B}}}){\mathds{1}}
in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Let
B,B^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}
be algebras with structures
(u_{B},m_{B})
and
(u_{B^{\prime}},m_{B^{\prime}})
respectively. A morphism
h:B\to B^{\prime}
is called an algebra homomorphism if the following commutative diagrams hold:

(5.21)

48.31079pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 48.31079pt\raise-32.01782pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B^{\prime}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

A subobject
(B,\iota)
of
A
is called a subalgebra, if
B
admits an algebra structure
(u_{B},m_{B})
such that
\iota:B\to A
is an algebra monomorphism. A condensable subalgebra
(B,\iota)
of
A
means
B
is also condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
be the lattice of fusion (full) subcategory of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
containing
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
. For
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
, we define
A^{\mathcal{B}}
as the image
\iota:B\to A
of
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
. We will prove in Proposition
5.5
that
(B,\iota)
is a subalgebra of
A
with the unit map
u_{B}:=\pi u_{A}
and multiplication
m_{B}:=\pi m_{A}(\iota{\otimes}\iota)
. We denote by
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\leq{\mathcal{B}}_{2}
if
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
is a fusion subcategory of
{\mathcal{B}}_{2}
. Let
(B_{1},\iota_{1})
,
(B_{2},\iota_{2})
be subalgebra of
A
. We will write
(B_{1},\iota_{1})\leq(B_{2},\iota_{2})
if
\iota_{1}=\iota_{2}h
for some algebra monomorphism
h:B_{1}\to B_{2}
, and
(B_{1},\iota_{1})\equiv(B_{2},\iota_{2})
if
h
is an algebra isomorphism. If
(B_{1},\iota_{1})\leq(B_{2},\iota_{2})
and
(B_{1},\iota_{1})\not\equiv(B_{2},\iota_{2})
, we will write
(B_{1},\iota_{1})<(B_{2},\iota_{2})
. The lattice of condensable subalgebras of
A
is denoted by
{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
. Similarly, one can define
(B_{1},\iota_{1})\leq(B_{2},\iota_{2})
as subobjects of
A
if
\iota_{1}=\iota_{2}h
for some monomorphism of
h:B_{1}\to B_{2}
of
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
. We first prove Proposition
5.5
, which asserts that
A^{\mathcal{B}}
is a subalgebra of
A
. Under the assumption
d(A^{\mathcal{B}})\neq 0
,
A^{\mathcal{B}}
is condensable.

For
y\in{\mathcal{C}}
, one can restrict the action of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
to
{\mathcal{B}}
on
{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)
, and we define

{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)^{\mathcal{B}}=\{f\in{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)\mid Yf=d_{A}(Y)f\text{ for all }Y\in{\mathcal{B}}\},

which is the isotypic component of
{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)
of the dimension character of
{\mathcal{B}}
. Note that
Yf=(Y\operatorname{id}_{A})\circ f
for any
f\in{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)
. Thus, for any
g:y\to y^{\prime}
and
f\in{\mathcal{C}}(y^{\prime},A)^{\mathcal{B}}
,

Y(f\circ g)=((Y\operatorname{id}_{A})\circ f)\circ g=d_{A}(Y)f\circ g

for any
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}
. So,
{\mathcal{C}}(-,A)^{\mathcal{B}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\operatorname{Vec}}
is a contarvariant linear functor.

The following lemma provides another definition of
A^{\mathcal{B}}
by the Yoneda lemma.

Lemma 5.2
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
,
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})=\iota\pi
the monic-epi factorization of
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
,
A^{\mathcal{B}}=(B,\iota)
as a subobject of
A
.
For any
y\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
\phi_{y}:{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)^{\mathcal{B}}\to{\mathcal{C}}(y,A^{\mathcal{B}})
is a natural isomorphism, where
\phi_{y}(f)=\pi f
. Moreover,
\iota\in{\mathcal{C}}(B,A)^{\mathcal{B}}
,
\pi\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})=\pi
and
\phi
is an isomorphism of linear functors.

Proof.

For any
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}
and
f\in{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)^{\mathcal{B}}
,
(Y\,\operatorname{id}_{A})\circ f=Yf=d_{A}(Y)\cdot f
. Thus,

\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})\circ f=\frac{1}{\dim({\mathcal{B}})}\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{B}})}}d_{A}(Y)(Y\,\operatorname{id}_{A})\circ f=\frac{1}{\dim({\mathcal{B}})}\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{B}})}}d_{A}(Y)^{2}f=f\,.

Since
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})=\iota\pi
,
f=\iota\pi f=\iota\phi_{y}(f)
, which implies
\phi_{y}
is injective. The
\mathbb{C}
-linearity of
\phi_{y}
is obvious.

We claim
\phi_{y}^{-1}(g)=\iota g
for any
g\in{\mathcal{C}}(y,B)
. Since
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})^{2}=\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
,
\iota\pi\iota\pi=\iota\pi
. Note that
\iota
is a monomorphism and
\pi
is an epimorphism. Therefore,

(5.22)

\iota\pi\iota=\iota,\quad\pi\iota\pi=\pi\quad\text{and}\quad\pi\iota=\operatorname{id}_{B}.

The first equality means
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})\iota=\iota
and hence
\iota\in{\mathcal{C}}(B,A)^{\mathcal{B}}
and so
\iota g\in{\mathcal{C}}(y,A)^{\mathcal{B}}
for any
g\in{\mathcal{C}}(y,B)
. It follows from the third equality of (
5.22
) that
\phi_{y}(\iota g)=f
for any
g\in{\mathcal{C}}(y,B)
, which proves the claim. Moreover, the second equality of (
5.22
) implies
\pi\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})=\pi
.

For
h\in{\mathcal{C}}(y,y^{\prime})
and
f\in{\mathcal{C}}(y^{\prime},A)^{\mathcal{B}}
,

\phi_{y^{\prime}}(f)h=(\pi f)h=\pi(fh)=\phi_{y}(fh)\,,

which means
\phi_{y}
is a natural in
y
.
∎

Lemma 5.3
.

For any
x_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}}
and
f_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}}(x_{i},A)
,
i=1,2
,
m_{A}(f_{1}{\otimes}f_{2})\in{\mathcal{C}}(x_{1}{\otimes}x_{2},A)
and

\psi(m_{A}\circ(f_{1}{\otimes}f_{2}))=\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2})\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x_{1}){\otimes}_{A}{\alpha}(x_{2}),{\mathds{1}}),

where
{\alpha}(x_{1}){\otimes\!_{A}}{\alpha}(x_{2})
is identified with
{\alpha}(x_{1}{\otimes}x_{2})
.

Proof.

Let
p_{A}:X{\otimes}Y\to X{\otimes}_{A}Y
denote the cokernel which defines the tensor product
X{\otimes}_{A}Y
for
X,Y\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}.
By the universal property of
p_{A}:{\alpha}(x_{1}){\otimes}{\alpha}(x_{2})\to{\alpha}(x_{1}){\otimes}_{A}{\alpha}(x_{2})
,
\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2})
is determined by the commutative trapezoid on the right side of the following diagram:

The morphism
p^{\prime}:{\alpha}(x_{1}){\otimes}{\alpha}(x_{2})\to{\alpha}(x_{1}{\otimes}x_{2})
on the left side of the diagram is defined by

which satisfies the universal property of
{\alpha}(x_{1}){\otimes}_{A}{\alpha}(x_{2})
. Thus, there exists an isomorphism
\tilde{p}^{\prime}:{\alpha}(x_{1}){\otimes}_{A}{\alpha}(x_{2})\to{\alpha}(x_{1}{\otimes}x_{2})
such that the left triangle of the preceding diagram commutes. To complete the proof, it suffices to show that

\psi(m_{A}(f_{1}{\otimes}f_{2}))\circ p^{\prime}=m_{A}\circ(\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}\psi(f_{2})).

x_{2}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}}}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}}=\psi(m_{A}(f_{1}{\otimes}f_{2}))\circ p^{\prime}\,.\qed

Since
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is a spherical fusion category, for any
X,Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
, the pairing
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle:{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}(Y,X){\otimes}{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}(X,Y)\to\mathbb{C}

defined by the composition of morphisms and taking categorical trace, i.e.
f{\otimes}g\mapsto\langle f,g\rangle:=\operatorname{tr}(f\circ g)
is a nondegenerate bilinear form. Now, we apply this to prove the following lemma.

Lemma 5.4
.

For any
x_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
f_{i}\in{\mathcal{C}}(x_{i},A)^{\mathcal{B}}
,
i=1,2
, we have

m_{A}\circ(f_{1}{\otimes}f_{2})\in{\mathcal{C}}(x_{1}{\otimes}x_{2},A)^{\mathcal{B}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
5.3
,

\psi(m_{A}\circ(f_{1}{\otimes}f_{2}))=\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2})\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x_{1}){\otimes}_{A}{\alpha}(x_{2}),{\mathds{1}}),

where
\psi(f_{i})\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x_{i}),{\mathds{1}})^{\mathcal{B}}
,
i=1,2
. We claim that
Y\cdot(\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2}))=d_{A}(Y)\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2})
for all
Y\in{\mathcal{B}}
. For any
g_{i}\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\mathds{1}},{\alpha}(x_{i}))
,

\langle g_{i},Y\psi(f_{i})\rangle=d_{A}(Y)\langle g_{i},\psi(f_{i})\rangle.

In terms of diagram, we have

\langle g_{i},Y\psi(f_{i})\rangle={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise g_{i}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise Thus, for
Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{B}})}
, we have

}{\mathcal{B}},

and hence

\displaystyle\langle g_{1}{\otimes}_{A}g_{2},Y(\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2}))\rangle={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern Y}$}}}}}}}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\,d_{A}(Y)\langle g_{1},\psi(f_{1})\rangle\langle g_{2},\psi(f_{2})\rangle\,.

This implies

Y(\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2}))=d_{A}(Y)\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2}),

and so
\psi(f_{1}){\otimes}_{A}\psi(f_{2})\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}({\alpha}(x_{1}{\otimes}x_{2}),{\mathds{1}})^{\mathcal{B}}
.
∎

Since
A
is a condensable algebra,
d(A)\neq 0
,
\theta_{A}=\operatorname{id}_{A}
and the section
\varepsilon_{A}:A\to{\mathds{1}}
of
u_{A}
defines the evaluation
\operatorname{ev}_{A}:A{\otimes}A\to{\mathds{1}}
given by
\operatorname{ev}_{A}=\varepsilon_{A}m_{A}
, the evaluation morphism for the left dual of
A
. In particular,
A=A^{*}
, and so
f^{*}\in{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
for any
f\in{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
.

Let us denote the morphism
\varepsilon_{A}
as

\textstyle{\scriptstyle A}
\textstyle{\circ}

. Then

\operatorname{ev}_{A}=\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 18.00696pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.00696pt\raise 24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle A}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 5.99304pt\raise 24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle A}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}{{}{{}{{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}}}}}}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}{{}}}}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{{}}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces}}}}}\,=\,\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 18.00696pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-18.00696pt\raise 24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle A}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 5.99304pt\raise 24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle A}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-6.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\circ}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}

It is unclear whether
d(A^{\mathcal{B}})\neq 0
, but it has to be if
{\mathcal{C}}
has positive dimensions. Under the assumption that
d(A^{\mathcal{B}})\neq 0
,
A^{\mathcal{B}}
is condensable as stated in the following lemma.

Proposition 5.5
.

The subobject
A^{\mathcal{B}}=(B,\iota)
is a subalgebra of
A
, with the unit map
u_{B}=\pi u_{A}
and multiplication
m_{B}=\pi m_{A}(\iota{\otimes}\iota)
, where
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})=\iota\pi
is the monic-epic factorization. In addition, if
d(B)\neq 0
, then
A^{\mathcal{B}}
is condensable, and hence
A^{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
.

Proof.

The hom-space
{\mathcal{C}}({\mathds{1}},A)
, spanned by the unit map
u_{A}:{\mathds{1}}\to A
, is an 1-dimensional representation of
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
associated with the dimension character, i.e.
Yu_{A}=d_{A}(Y)u_{A}
for all
Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
. In particular,
u_{A}\in{\mathcal{C}}({\mathds{1}},A)^{\mathcal{B}}
and
u_{A}=\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})u_{A}=\iota\pi u_{A}=\iota u_{B}
.

It follows from Lemmas
5.2
and
5.4
that
m_{A}(\iota{\otimes}\iota)\in{\mathcal{C}}(B{\otimes}B,A)^{\mathcal{B}}
. So,

(5.23)

m_{A}(\iota{\otimes}\iota)=\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})m_{A}(\iota{\otimes}\iota)=\iota\pi m_{A}(\iota{\otimes}\iota)=\iota m_{B}\,.

This proves
(B,\iota)
is a subalgebra of
A
.

Since
A
is a condensable algebra, and
\iota:B\to A
is a subalgebra,
\theta_{B}=\operatorname{id}_{B}
. To prove that last assertion the lemma, it suffices to show that
B
is rigid in the sense of
KO02
if
d(B)\neq 0
. We need to show that
(\varepsilon_{B}m_{B}{\otimes}\operatorname{id}_{B^{*}})(\operatorname{id}_{B}{\otimes}\operatorname{coev}_{B})
is an isomorphism where
\varepsilon_{B}
is the section of
u_{B}
. Since
u_{B}=\pi u_{A}
,
\varepsilon_{B}=\varepsilon_{A}\iota
. Note that
\varepsilon_{B}m_{B}=\varepsilon_{A}m_{A}(\iota{\otimes}\iota)
.
In terms of diagram, we have

(\varepsilon_{B}m_{B}{\otimes}\operatorname{id}_{B^{*}})(\operatorname{id}_{B}{\otimes}\operatorname{coev}_{B})=\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.59146pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.59146pt\raise 42.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle B}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{{}{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-0.1915pt\raise 31.20007pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle B}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 24.71822pt\raise-9.60004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle B^{*}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-9.00696pt\raise 3.60004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle A}$}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-12.89996pt\raise 18.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.02025pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\iota}$}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 3.89996pt\raise 18.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.02025pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\iota}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-9.60004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\circ}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}=\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 14.59146pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.59146pt\raise 42.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle B}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}\lx@xy@spline@}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern 19.5pt\raise 8.39996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.92995pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\iota^{*}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-0.00696pt\raise 20.39996pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle A}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 24.71822pt\raise-9.60004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle B^{*}}$}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-9.00696pt\raise 3.60004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle A}$}}}}}{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-12.89996pt\raise 18.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.02025pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\iota}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-2.5pt\raise-9.60004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.22223pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\circ}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces}}}}}=\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 16.68175pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-14.59146pt\raise 42.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle B}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-12.89996pt\raise 22.79993pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.02025pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\iota}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{{{\hbox{\framed@@{0.0pt}}}}\hbox{\kern-12.89996pt\raise 9.60004pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.92995pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle\iota^{*}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-16.68175pt\raise-10.79993pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.67209pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle B^{*}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\,.

By Lemma
3.9
,
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})^{*}=\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
, and so
\pi^{*}\iota^{*}=\iota\pi
. By the uniqueness of monic-epi factorization of
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
, there exists an isomorphism
h:B\to B^{*}
such that

h\pi=\iota^{*}
and
h\pi^{*}=\iota
. Thus

\iota^{*}\iota=h\pi\iota=h\operatorname{id}_{B}=h\,.

Therefore,
B
is rigid and hence condensable.
∎

Now we state and prove our major Theorem II.

Theorem 5.6

(Galois correspondence)
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a pseudounitary modular tensor category, and
A
a condensable balgebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
. Then the assignment
{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}\to{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
,
{\mathcal{B}}\mapsto A^{\mathcal{B}}
, defines an order-reversing bijection, whose inverse is given by the assignment
{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}\to{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
,
(B,\iota)\mapsto({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}
. In particular,
{\mathcal{B}}=({\mathcal{C}}_{A^{\mathcal{B}}}^{0})_{A}
for any
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
and

\dim({\mathcal{B}})=\frac{\dim({\mathcal{C}})}{d(A)d(B)}\,.

Proof.

We divide the proof of Theorem
5.6
into four major steps.

(i) For any
(B,\iota)\in{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
, we claim that
A^{({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}}\equiv(B,\iota)
as subalgebra of
A
.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}=({\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0})_{A}.
Since
A
is a condensable algebra in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
, we can apply the algebra isomorphism
\rho_{B}:e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{A}}}{K({\mathcal{B}})}\to{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A,A)
that was established in Theorem
4.1
, where the
B
-module structure of
A
is determined by
(B,\iota)
. In particular,
\rho_{B}(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
is a primitive central idempotent of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A,A)
. Let
\rho_{B}(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})=\iota^{\prime}\pi^{\prime}
be the monic-epi factorization of
\rho_{B}(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
. Since
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(B,A)
is 1-dimensional and both
\iota^{\prime},\iota
are monomorphism in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(B,A)
,
\iota^{\prime}=\lambda\iota
for some
\lambda\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
. The uniqueness of the monic-epi factorization of
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{B}})
implies that there exists an isomorphism
h:B\to B
such that the diagram commutes:

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 13.41185pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 52.35704pt\raise 4.50694pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.50694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.7934pt\raise-14.75pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.43056pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{h}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.7934pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\vskip 3.0pt plus 1.0pt minus 1.0pt}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\hbox{\kern 1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\hbox{\kern-1.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}}}{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 12.0594pt\raise-25.43552pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\pi^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 30.75pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 51.0046pt\raise-25.43552pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.8978pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 68.83679pt\raise-31.33331pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\,.

Therefore,
h=\lambda^{-1}\operatorname{id}_{B}
. Let
(u_{B}^{\prime},m_{B}^{\prime})
be the algebra structure of
(B,\iota^{\prime})
. It is immediate to see that
u_{B}^{\prime}=\lambda^{-1}u_{B}
and
m_{B}^{\prime}=\lambda m_{B}
, and direct verification shows that
h:(B,\iota)\to(B,\iota^{\prime})
is an isomorphism of algebras. Therefore,
(B,\iota)\equiv(B,\iota^{\prime})
as subalgebras of
A
. This completes the proof of the claim.

(ii) The assignment
(B,\iota)\mapsto({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}
is strictly order-reversing.

If
(B_{1},\iota_{1})<(B_{2},\iota_{2})
in
{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
, then
({\mathcal{C}}_{B_{2}}^{0})_{A}\subseteq({\mathcal{C}}_{B_{1}}^{0})_{A}
. However,

\operatorname{FPdim}(({\mathcal{C}}_{B_{2}}^{0})_{A})=\frac{\operatorname{FPdim}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}{\operatorname{FPdim}(B_{2})}<\frac{\operatorname{FPdim}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}{\operatorname{FPdim}(B_{1})}=\operatorname{FPdim}(({\mathcal{C}}_{B_{1}}^{0})_{A})\,,

and so
({\mathcal{C}}_{B_{2}}^{0})_{A}\subsetneq({\mathcal{C}}_{B_{1}}^{0})_{A}
.

(iii) The assignment
{\mathcal{B}}\mapsto A^{\mathcal{B}}
is also strictly order-reversing.

Suppose
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}<{\mathcal{B}}_{2}
in
{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
. Then
{K({\mathcal{B}}_{1})}
is a proper subalgebra of
{K({\mathcal{B}}_{2})}
, and so
e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}{K({\mathcal{B}}_{1})}\subsetneq e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}{K({\mathcal{B}}_{2})}
. In particular,
e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}=e_{1}^{\mathcal{A}}e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}\neq e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}=e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}
and
e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}=e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}
. Since
\rho:e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}{K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}\to{\mathcal{C}}(A,A)
is an isomorphism of semisimple algebras,
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}})\neq\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}})
and
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}})\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}})=\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}})
. Let
A^{{\mathcal{B}}_{i}}=(B_{i},\iota_{i})
and
\pi_{i}
the coimage of
\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{i}})
for
i=1,2
. Then
\iota_{1}\pi_{1}\iota_{2}\pi_{2}=\iota_{2}\pi_{2}
. Since
\pi_{2}
is an epimorphism, we have
\iota_{1}\pi_{1}\iota_{2}=\iota_{2}
. Let
h:=\pi_{1}\iota_{2}
. The preceding equality implies
h:B_{2}\to B_{1}
is a monomorphism and
\iota_{1}h=\iota_{2}
.

We claim that
h
is an algebra monomorphism, with the algebra structures
(u_{B_{i}},m_{B_{i}})
defined in Definition
5.1
. Since
\iota_{2}u_{B_{2}}=u_{A}
, we have

hu_{B_{2}}=\pi_{1}\iota_{2}u_{B_{2}}=\pi_{1}u_{A}=u_{B_{1}}\,.

Since
m_{B_{i}}=\pi_{i}m_{A}(\iota_{i}{\otimes}\iota_{i})
, we find

m_{B_{1}}(h{\otimes}h)=\pi_{1}m_{A}(\iota_{1}h{\otimes}\iota_{1}h)=\pi_{1}m_{A}(\iota_{2}{\otimes}\iota_{2})=\pi_{1}\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}})m_{A}(\iota_{2}{\otimes}\iota_{2})

where the last equality follows from (
5.23
) proved in Proposition
5.5
. Thus,

m_{B_{1}}(h{\otimes}h)=\pi_{1}\iota_{2}\pi_{2}m_{A}(\iota_{2}{\otimes}\iota_{2})=hm_{B_{2}}\,.

This proves our claim.

If
(B_{1},\iota_{1})\equiv(B_{2},\iota_{2})
, then
\operatorname{FPdim}(B_{1})=\operatorname{FPdim}(B_{2})
or
h:B_{2}\to B_{1}
is an algebra isomorphism. Let
h^{\prime}=\pi_{2}\iota_{1}
. Since
\iota_{2}\pi_{2}\iota_{1}\pi_{1}=\iota_{2}\pi_{2}
and
\iota_{2}
is a monomorphism,
we have
h^{\prime}\pi_{1}=\pi_{2}
and

h^{\prime}h=\pi_{2}\iota_{1}\pi_{1}\iota_{2}=\pi_{2}\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}})\iota_{2}=\pi_{2}\iota_{2}=\operatorname{id}_{B_{2}},

Therefore,
h^{\prime}=h^{-1}
and

\iota_{2}\pi_{2}=\iota_{2}h^{-1}\pi_{1}=\iota_{1}\pi_{1}
. This implies

\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}})=\rho(e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}})\,,

which is a contradiction. Therefore,
(B_{1},\iota_{1})\not\equiv(B_{2},\iota_{2})
or
A^{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}}<A^{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}
in
{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
.

(iv) For
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
, we claim that
{\mathcal{B}}=({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}
where
(B,\iota)=A^{\mathcal{B}}
, and hence

\dim({\mathcal{B}})=\frac{\dim({\mathcal{C}})}{d(B)d(A)}\,.

Since the morphism
\iota\in{\mathcal{C}}(B,A)^{\mathcal{B}}
, for any
X\in{\mathcal{B}}
, we have

\displaystyle d_{A}(X)\operatorname{id}_{B}=\pi(d_{A}(X)\iota)=\pi\circ X\iota=\frac{1}{d(A)}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 22.75095pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\ignorespaces}{\ignorespaces}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}}}}}}}}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{{}{}{{{}{}{}{}{{{{{}}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}{{{}}{{{}}}{{}}{{}}}}}}}}}}}}}}}{{}{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern
