Let
X^{\prime}
be the sum of local
B
-submodules of
X
. According to
(
KO02
, Lem. 4.3)
,

\frac{1}{d(B)}\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 2.39996pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 9.04898pt\raise 42.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle X}$}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.60004pt\raise 12.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\hbox{\kern 6.0pt\vrule height=3.0pt,depth=3.0pt,width=0.0pt}}$}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}\lx@xy@spline@}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}\lx@xy@spline@}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}\lx@xy@spline@}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}\lx@xy@spline@}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{}{{}{}}{{}}{}{{}}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}\lx@xy@spline@}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}\lx@xy@spline@}{{}{}{}\lx@xy@spline@}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{{}{}{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{{}{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}\ignorespaces{}}{{}{}{}}{}}}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 20.8085pt\raise 30.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.39168pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\scriptstyle B}$}}}}}}}}}}

is the projection onto
X^{\prime}
, and its trace is equal to
\frac{d(B)d(X^{\prime})}{d(A)}
. Thus,
d(X)=d(X^{\prime})
. Since
X^{\prime}
is a subobject of
X
and the dimension of any simple object of
{\mathcal{C}}
is positive,
X=X^{\prime}
. Therefore,
X
is a local
B
-module or
{\mathcal{B}}\subseteq({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}
.

If
{\mathcal{B}}<({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}
, by (i) and (iii), we have

(B,\iota)=A^{\mathcal{B}}<A^{({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}}\equiv(B,\iota)\,,

which is a contradiction. Therefore,
{\mathcal{B}}=({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}
.
∎

As a consequence of the preceding theorem of fusion action Galois correspondence, we establish a more general version of Schur-Weyl duality.

Theorem 5.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a pseudounitary MTC,
A
a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
,
{\mathcal{B}}
a fusion subcategory of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
containing
{\mathcal{A}}={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
, and
A^{\mathcal{B}}=(B,\iota)
. Then

(i)

For any
X\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})
, if
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)\neq 0
, then
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)
is an irreducible
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
-module.

(ii)

For any simple objects
X,Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
, the
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
-modules
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)
and
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(Y,A)
are isomorphic if and only if
X\cong Y
as
B
-modules.

(iii)

e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}K({\mathcal{B}})\cong{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A,A)
as
\mathbb{C}
-algebras.

(iv)

For any simple object
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
,
\rho(e^{\mathcal{B}}_{X^{*}}){{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A,A)\cong{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A_{X},A_{X})
as
\mathbb{C}
-algebra, where
A_{X}
is the isotypic component of
X
in
A
, and
e_{X}^{\mathcal{B}}
is the central idempotent of
K({\mathcal{B}})
corresponding to the simple
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
-module
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)
.

Proof.

Since
({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}={\mathcal{B}}
by Theorem
5.6
and
({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}^{0}={\mathcal{A}}
, one can apply Theorem
4.1
to the condensable algebra
A
in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
. Now the statements (i), (ii) and (iii) follow directly. For any
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
,
{\tilde{\alpha}}(X)=X{\otimes}_{B}A
and so

[{\tilde{\alpha}}(X),A]_{{\mathcal{B}}}=[X,A]_{{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}}\,.

It follows from the proof of Theorem
4.1
that the set indecomposable central idempotents of
e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}K({\mathcal{B}})
is

\{e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{A}}}e_{X}^{\mathcal{B}}\mid X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}},\ [X,A]_{{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}}\neq 0\},

and the kernel of ring epimorphism
\rho:K({\mathcal{B}})\to{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A,A)
is
(1-e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}})K_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{B}})
. Thus,
\rho(e_{X^{*}}^{\mathcal{B}})
is an indecomposable central idempotent of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A,A)
if
[X,A]_{{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}}\neq 0
. Moreover,
\rho(e_{X^{*}}^{\mathcal{B}})
is a projection of
A
onto
A_{X}
. Thus,
\rho(e_{X^{*}}^{\mathcal{B}}){{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A,A)\cong{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(A_{X},A_{X})
as
\mathbb{C}
-algebras.
∎

Since the unit object in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
is
B
, the multiplicity space
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)
can be identified with
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)B
, and we have

A=\sum_{X\in{\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})}}{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A){\otimes}_{B}X

which is a categorical generalization of Schur-Weyl duality of the algebra
A
in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
.

Proof of Theorem
III
.

Now the theorem follows from Theorems
4.1
and
5.6
immediately
with
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{M}_{U}}
.
∎

6.
Galois Correspondence via relative centers

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a modular tensor category with a Lagrangian algebra
A
. Then
{\mathcal{C}}=Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
. By
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.1)
, there exists an order-reversing bijective correspondence between
{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
and
{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
. In this section, we recall the result of
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.1)
with additional details which is relevant to our discussion, and apply to the case that
A
is not a Lagrangian algebra of
{\mathcal{C}}
. We further show that the condensable subalgebra
(B,\iota)
of
A
corresponding to the fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
is indeed isomorphic to
A^{\mathcal{B}}
as an object in
{\mathcal{C}}
. In this regard, Galois correspondence between
{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
and
{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
can also be established from
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.1)
. Following the approach of
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.1)
, the correspondence for any condensable algebra
A
in a modular tensor category
{\mathcal{C}}
was proved in
(
LKW
, Thm. 3.5)
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a pseudounitary MTC. Suppose
A\in{\mathcal{C}}
is a Lagrangian algebra. Then
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is a spherical fusion category and
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})={\mathcal{C}}
. Now, we consider any fusion (full) subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
, and let
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
be the relative center of
{\mathcal{B}}
in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
. Recall that the objects of
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
are pairs
(X,\sigma_{X,-})
where
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
and
\sigma_{X,V}:X{\otimes}V\to V{\otimes}X
for
V\in{\mathcal{B}}
is an isomorphism natural at
V
and it satisfies the half-braiding conditions. In particular,
Z({\mathcal{B}})^{\operatorname{rev}}
is a tensor subcategory of
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
.

The forgetful functor
F:Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\to{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
can be factorized as

Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\xrightarrow{F_{\mathcal{B}}}Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\xrightarrow{{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{B}}}{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}\,,

which is a composition of forgetful functors. Let
I_{\mathcal{B}}:Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\to Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
and
{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}:{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}\to Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
be the right adjoints of the forgetful functors
F_{\mathcal{B}},{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{B}}
respectively. It is clear that
I_{\mathcal{B}}{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}=I
, the right adjoint of
F
.

Since
{\mathds{1}}
is an algebra in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
,
{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
is an algebra in
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
. It was shown in
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.10)
that
I_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})=B
is a condensable algebra of
{\mathcal{C}}
and is considered as a subalgebra of
A=I({\mathds{1}})
via the embedding
I_{\mathcal{B}}(u^{\prime}):B\to A
, where
u^{\prime}:{\mathds{1}}\to{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
is unit map of the algebra
{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
. The following theorem has been proved in
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.10)
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
{\mathcal{C}}
be a pseudounitary MTC and
A
a Lagrangian algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
.
The assignment for any fusion subcategories
{\mathcal{B}}\subseteq{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
to the condensable subalgebra
B:=I_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})\subseteq I({\mathds{1}})
defines an order reversing one-to-one correspondence between
{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
and
{\mathcal{L}_{\operatorname{alg}}(A)}
, and
\operatorname{FPdim}(B)\operatorname{FPdim}({\mathcal{B}})=\operatorname{FPdim}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
. Moreover,
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
is tensor equivalent to
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})_{B}
, and the subcategory
Z({\mathcal{B}})^{\operatorname{rev}}
of
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
is mapped to
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})_{B}^{0}
under this equivalence.

To archive our goal, we follow a similar approach as the proof of
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.10)
but
A
is an arbitrary condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
. Since
Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})=({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}\boxtimes{\alpha}^{+}({\mathcal{C}})
as modular tensor categories, one can consider the composition

F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}:={\mathcal{C}}\xrightarrow{{\alpha}_{A}^{+}}Z({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\xrightarrow{F_{\mathcal{B}}}Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})

which is obviously a central functor. Let
I^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}:Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\to{\mathcal{C}}
be the right adjoint of
F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}
. By
(
DMNO
, Lem. 3.5)
,
B=I^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
is a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
and
{\mathcal{C}}_{B}
is equivalent as tensor categories to
\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}
, the smallest tensor subcategory of
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
containing the image of
F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}
. One can verify directly that the composition
{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{B}}F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}={\alpha}_{A}
, where
{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{B}}:Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})\to{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
denotes the forgetful functor. Since
{\alpha}_{A}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is surjective, so is
{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{B}}:\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}\to{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
.

Let
{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}:{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}\to Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
be the right adjoint of
{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{B}}
. Then the composition
I^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}
is the right adjoint of
{\alpha}_{A}:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
, and so
I^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}
is isomorphic to the forgetful functor
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}\to{\mathcal{C}}
.

If
{\mathcal{B}}={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
,
{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{B}}:\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}\to{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is an equivalence. To see this, one should first notice that the braiding
R_{X,Y}
in
{\mathcal{C}}
for
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
and
Y\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
can be descended to an isomorphism
{\tilde{R}}_{X,Y}:X{\otimes}_{A}Y\to Y{\otimes}_{A}X
of
A
-modules, and
(X,{\tilde{R}}_{X,-})\in Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
. These pairs
(X,{\tilde{R}}_{X,-})
form a tensor subcategory
\widetilde{{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}}
of
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
, and
\widetilde{{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}}=\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}
. Therefore,
{\tilde{F}}_{\mathcal{B}}:\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}\to{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is an equivalence of tensor categories and
I^{+}_{{\mathcal{B}}}({\mathds{1}})=A.

The preceding paragraph and the following lemma are elaborations of
(
DMNO
, Remark 4.12)
.

Lemma 6.2
.

For any fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
containing
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
,
B=I^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
is a condensable subalgebra of
A
, and
A
is a condensable algebra in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
. Moreover,
({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}={\mathcal{B}}
and
Z({\mathcal{B}})
is equivalent to
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}\boxtimes({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}
as braided tensor categories.

Proof.

Let
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
. By
(
DMNO
, Thm. 4.10)
,
B:=I^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
is considered as a condensable subalgebra of
A
via the algebra embedding
\iota:B\to A
given by
\iota:=I^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}(u)
, where
u:{\mathds{1}}\to{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
is the unit map of the algebra
{\tilde{I}}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathds{1}})
in
Z_{\mathcal{B}}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
. In particular,
A
is a condensable algebra in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
.

By
(
DMNO
, Lem. 3.5)
,
\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}
is equivalent to
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}
as fusion categories via the internal hom-functor
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}({\mathds{1}},Y)
for
Y\in\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}
(cf.
(
Ost03Mod
, Thm. 3.1)
). By
(
Ost03Mod
, Lem 3.3)
, for
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
,
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}({\mathds{1}},F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}(x))\cong x{\otimes}\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}({\mathds{1}},{\mathds{1}})\cong x{\otimes}B
as
B
-modules. So, the functor
F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}:{\mathcal{C}}\to\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}
is identified with the induction functor
-{\otimes}B:{\mathcal{C}}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{B}
, and
{\tilde{F}}_{B}:\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
is identified with
-{\otimes}_{B}A:{\mathcal{C}}_{B}\to{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
. Moreover,
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
is identified with the subcategory
Z({\mathcal{B}})^{\operatorname{rev}}\cap\overline{F^{+}_{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{C}})}
(cf. Theorem
6.1
). Therefore,
({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}=\overline{{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}{\otimes}_{B}A}
is a subcategory of
{\mathcal{B}}
. Since

\operatorname{FPdim}(({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A})=\frac{\operatorname{FPdim}({\mathcal{C}})/\operatorname{FPdim}(B)^{2}}{\operatorname{FPdim}(A)/\operatorname{FPdim}(B)}=\frac{\operatorname{FPdim}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}{\operatorname{FPdim}(B)}=\operatorname{FPdim}({\mathcal{B}}),

we find
({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}={\mathcal{B}}
.

By
(
DMNO
, Cor. 3.30)
,
Z({\mathcal{B}})=Z(({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A})
is equivalent to
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}\boxtimes(({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}^{0})^{\operatorname{rev}}
. It is clear that
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
is a tensor subcategory of
({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}^{0}
. Since

\operatorname{FPdim}(({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}^{0})=\frac{\dim({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})}{d_{B}(A)^{2}}=\frac{\operatorname{FPdim}({\mathcal{C}})}{\operatorname{FPdim}(A)^{2}}=\operatorname{FPdim}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}),

we have
(({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}^{0}={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
, and the last assertion follows.
∎

Since
A\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
, one can consider its irreducible decomposition

A\cong\sum_{X\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})}[A,X]_{{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}}\,X\cong\sum_{X\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})}{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A){\otimes}_{B}X

in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
, where the
\mathbb{C}
-linear space
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)
is identified with
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A){\mathds{1}}_{{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}}B
in the second isomorphism. Since
({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})_{A}={\mathcal{B}}
by Lemma
6.2
,
K_{0}({\mathcal{B}})
acts on
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)
for any
X\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
by Definition
3.5
(with
{\mathcal{C}}
being replaced by
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
).

The indecomposable idempotents of
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})
indexed by
X\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})
is defined as

e_{X}^{{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}=\frac{d_{B}(X)}{\dim({\widehat{\mathcal{B}}})}\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\widehat{\mathcal{B}}})}S^{{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}_{X,Y}Y^{*}\,.

Since
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
is embedded into
Z({\mathcal{B}})
via
{\tilde{\alpha}}^{+}_{A}:{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}\to Z({\mathcal{B}})
,

e_{X}^{\mathcal{B}}:=\frac{d_{B}(X)}{\dim({\widehat{\mathcal{B}}})}\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\widehat{\mathcal{B}}})}S^{{\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}}_{X,Y^{*}}{\tilde{\alpha}}^{+}(Y)

is a central idempotent of
K({\mathcal{B}})
whenever
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(X,A)\neq 0
. Same as before, we let
{\mathcal{A}}=({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})^{\operatorname{rev}}
, and

e_{\mathds{1}}^{{\mathcal{A}}}=\frac{1}{\dim({\mathcal{A}})}\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{A}})}d_{A}(Y)Y\,.

Since
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}\subseteq{\mathcal{B}}
,
e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}
is a central idempotent of
K_{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{B}})
.

Now, we can apply Theorem
4.1
to this setting to obtain the following “Galois correspondence” between fusion subcategories of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
and condensable subalgebra of
A
.

Corollary 6.3
.

The condensable subalgebra
B
of
A
determined by
{\mathcal{B}}\in{\mathcal{L}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}
is isomorphic to
A^{\mathcal{B}}
as objects in
{\mathcal{C}}
.

Proof.

Let
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})
such that
{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)\neq 0
. Then

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)

\displaystyle={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}(x{\otimes}A,A)={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}(x{\otimes}B{\otimes}_{B}A,A)={{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}(x{\otimes}B,A)

\displaystyle=\,\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}})}{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}(x{\otimes}B,Y){\otimes}{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}(Y,A)

\displaystyle=\,\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})}{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}(x{\otimes}B,Y){\otimes}{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(Y,A)

\displaystyle=\,\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})}{\mathcal{C}}(x,Y){\otimes}{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(Y,A).

The second last equality holds because
A\in{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}
and so
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}}(Y,A)=0
if
Y\not\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}})
. Since
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(Y,A)
is an irreducible representation of
K({\mathcal{B}})
for
{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(Y,A)\neq 0
,

{\mathcal{C}}(x,A)^{\mathcal{B}}={\mathcal{C}}(x,B){\otimes}{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}(B,A)\cong{\mathcal{C}}(x,B)

as
\mathbb{C}
-spaces since
[B,A]_{{{\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0}}}=1
. Now, we have

B=\sum_{x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}})}{\mathcal{C}}(x,B)x={\mathcal{C}}(x,A)^{\mathcal{B}}x=A^{\mathcal{B}}\,.

∎

7.
Examples of fusion actions on VOAs

We discuss several examples of fusion actions in terms of orbifolds. In particular, we show in these examples that iterated orbifolds of group actions on VOAs are invariants of actions of some weakly integral fusion categories.

7.1.
Orbifold theory

Let
A
be a simple VOA. Let
G
be a finite automorphism group of
A
. It follows from
DLMcomp1996
that
A^{G}
is a simple vertex operator subalgebra of
A
and
A
has a Schur-Weyl type duality decomposition

A=\oplus_{\lambda\in\operatorname{Irr}(G)}W_{\lambda}\otimes A_{\lambda}

where
W_{\lambda}
is the irreducible
G
-module with character
\lambda
and the multiplicity spaces
A_{\lambda}
are inequivalent irreducible
A^{G}
-modules. In addition, there is a one-to-one correspondence between the subgroups of
G
and the sub VOAs of
A
containing
A^{G}
by sending subgroup
H
to
A^{H}

DM1997
;
HMT1999
. The tensor product of two modules
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
of a VOA
A
will be denoted by
V_{1}{\otimes}V_{2}
instead of the traditional notation
V_{1}\boxtimes V_{1}
. If
A_{1},A_{2}
are VOAs, then so is their tensor product
A_{1}{\otimes}A_{2}
over
\mathbb{C}
, and
{\mathcal{M}_{A_{1}{\otimes}A_{2}}}
is equivalent to the Deligne product
{\mathcal{M}_{A_{1}}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{M}_{A_{2}}}
.

We now explain that the group action of
G
on
A
is the fusion action in this paper. For this purpose, we need to assume that
A^{G}
is rational and
C_{2}
-cofinite. Then the
A^{G}
-module category
{\mathcal{C}}
is a modular tensor category, and
A
is a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}.

Theorem 7.1
.

The action of
\overline{K}=e_{\mathds{1}}^{\mathcal{A}}K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
on
A
is equivalent to the action of
G
on
A
, where
{\mathcal{A}}={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}
.

Proof.

By
EGNO

{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
is a
G
-graded fusion category

{\mathcal{C}}_{A}=\bigoplus_{g\in G}{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(g)

and

{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}=\bigcup_{g\in G}\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A}(g))

where
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(g)
is the category of
g
-twisted
A
-modules (see
(
DLXY2024
, Lemma 3.3)
,
(
McRae2021
, Proposition 2.4)
). Note that
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(1)={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}}.

We claim that

\frac{1}{d_{A}(X)}X=\frac{1}{d_{A}(Y)}Y
in
\overline{K}
for any
X,Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A}(g)).

Since

X\otimes Y^{*}=\sum_{W\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(1)}N_{X,Y^{*}}^{W}W,

d_{A}(X)d_{A}(Y)=\sum_{W\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(1)}N_{X,Y^{*}}^{W}d_{A}(W)

and
W=d_{A}(W)
in
\overline{K}
for
W\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(1)
.
We see that

\frac{1}{d_{A}(X)}X\cdot\frac{1}{d_{A}(Y)}Y^{*}=\frac{1}{d_{A}(X)d_{A}(Y)}\sum_{W\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(1)}N_{X,Y^{*}}^{W}W=1.

That is,
\frac{1}{d_{A}(X)}X\in\overline{K}
is invertible and
\frac{1}{d_{A}(X)}X=\frac{1}{d_{A}(Y)}Y.

For each
g\in G
we fix a simple
X_{g}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{A}(g)
and use the same notation for its image in
\overline{K}.
Set
\bar{g}=\frac{X_{g}}{d_{A}(X_{g})}.
Then
\overline{G}=\{\bar{g}\mid g\in G\}\subset\overline{K}
is a group isomorphic to
G
and
\overline{K}=\oplus_{g\in G}\mathbb{C}X_{g}
is isomorphic to the group algebra
\mathbb{C}[G].

It remains to prove that the actions of the groups
\overline{G}
and
G
on
W_{\lambda}={\mathcal{C}}(A_{\lambda},A)
are equivalent for all
\lambda\in\operatorname{Irr}(G)
. Recall from Section 4 that

\phi_{W_{\lambda}}=\sum_{Y\in{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}}\operatorname{Tr}(Y,W_{\lambda})Y^{*}=\sum_{g\in G}\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}(g))}\operatorname{Tr}(Y,W_{\lambda})Y^{*}

for any
\lambda\in\operatorname{Irr}(G).
Since
Y=d_{A}(Y)\bar{g}
for any
Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}(g))
, we see that

\sum_{Y\in\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}(g))}\operatorname{Tr}(Y,W_{\lambda})Y^{*}=\dim({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}^{\hskip 1.0pt0}})\operatorname{Tr}(\bar{g},W_{\lambda})\bar{g}^{-1}.

Thus

\frac{\dim({W_{\lambda}})}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\phi_{W_{\lambda}}=\frac{\dim(W_{\lambda})}{o(G)}\sum_{g\in G}\operatorname{Tr}(\bar{g},W_{\lambda})\bar{g}^{-1}.

Noting from
DJX2013
that
d(A_{\lambda})=\dim(W_{\lambda}).

It follows from (
4.15
) that

\frac{\dim(W_{\lambda})}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{W_{\lambda}},W_{\lambda})=\dim(W_{\lambda}).

Since
\phi_{W_{\lambda}}=0
on
W_{\mu}
if
\mu\neq\lambda
, we see immediately that the indecomposable central elements

\frac{\dim({W_{\lambda}})}{\dim({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}\phi_{W_{\lambda}}=\frac{\dim(W_{\lambda})}{o(G)}\sum_{g\in G}\lambda(g)g^{-1}

in
\overline{K}=\mathbb{C}[\overline{G}]=\mathbb{C}[G]
and the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
G
be a finite automorphism group of a simple VOA
A
such that
A^{G}
has a rational,
C_{2}
-cofinite subVOA
B
and
{\mathcal{M}_{B}}
is pseudounitary. Then
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
contains a
G
-graded fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
with
{\mathcal{B}}(1)={\mathcal{M}_{A}}
, where
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{M}_{B}}
. In particular, if
A
is holomorphic, then
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
contains
\operatorname{Vec}_{G}^{\omega}
as a fusion subcategory for some
\omega\in Z^{3}(G,\mathbb{C}^{\times})
.

Proof.

By the Galois correspondence (Theorem
5.6
) that
{\mathcal{B}}=({\mathcal{M}_{A^{G}}})_{A}=({\mathcal{C}}_{A^{G}}^{0})_{A}
is a fusion subcategory of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
. It follows from the proof Theorem
7.1
that
{\mathcal{B}}
is a
G
-graded fusion category with
{\mathcal{B}}(1)={\mathcal{M}_{A}}
. The last assertion is an immediate consequence of the fact that
\operatorname{Vec}={\mathcal{M}_{A}}
when
A
is holomorphic.
∎

Proposition 7.3
.

Let
A
be a holomorphic
VOA
and
A=A_{0}\supset A_{1}\supset\cdots\supset A_{n}
be simple subVOAs of
A
such that
A_{n}
is rational,
C_{2}
-cofinite of CFT type and
the weight of any irreducible
A_{n}
-module is positive except
A_{n}
itself. Suppose there exist finite subgroups
G_{i}
of
{\rm Aut}(A_{i})
such that
A_{i+1}=A_{i}^{G_{i}}
for
i=0,\dots,n-1
. Then
{\mathcal{B}}_{i+1}=({\mathcal{M}_{A_{i+1}}})_{A}
is a
G_{i}
-extension of
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
and

{\mathcal{B}}_{0}=\operatorname{Vec}\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\subset\cdots{\mathcal{B}}_{n}={{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}

where
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{M}_{A_{n}}}.
In particular,
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is a nilpotent category, and
\operatorname{FPdim}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})=|G_{0}|\cdots|G_{n-1}|
.

Proof.

From the assumption we know that
{\mathcal{M}_{A_{n}}}
is pseudounitary. By the preceding lemma,
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
is a
G_{0}
-extension of
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
. Assume that
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
is a
G_{i-1}
-extension of
{\mathcal{B}}_{i-1}
for some integer
i\geq 1
. By the preceding lemma,
({\mathcal{M}_{A_{i+1}}})_{A_{i}}=({\mathcal{C}}_{A_{i+1}}^{0})_{A_{i}}=\bigoplus_{g\in G_{i}}({\mathcal{M}_{A_{i+1}}})_{A_{i}}(g)
is a
G_{i}
-extension of
{\mathcal{M}_{A_{i}}}={\mathcal{C}}_{A_{i}}^{0}
.
Let
{\mathcal{B}}_{i+1}(g)
denote the full subcategory of
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
in which each simple object is a summand of
X{\otimes}_{A_{i}}A
for some
X\in({\mathcal{M}_{A_{i+1}}})_{A_{i}}(g)
. In particular,
X{\otimes}_{A_{i}}A\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i+1}(g)
for any
X\in({\mathcal{M}_{A_{i+1}}})_{A_{i}}(g).
Obviously,
{\mathcal{B}}_{i+1}(1)=({\mathcal{M}_{A_{i}}})_{A}={\mathcal{B}}_{i}
, and
A
is a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{M}_{A_{i}}}
. If
Y\in({\mathcal{M}_{A_{i+1}}})_{A_{i}}(h)
, then

(X{\otimes}_{A_{i}}A){\otimes}_{A}(Y{\otimes}_{A_{i}}A)\cong(X{\otimes}_{A_{i}}Y){\otimes}_{A_{i}}A\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i+1}(gh)

and

(Y{\otimes}_{A_{i}}A)^{*}\cong Y^{*}{\otimes}_{A_{i}}A\in{\mathcal{B}}_{i+1}(h^{-1})\,.

Therefore,
{\mathcal{B}}_{i+1}
is a
G_{i}
-extension of
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
and
\operatorname{FPdim}({\mathcal{B}}_{i+1})=|G_{i}|\operatorname{FPdim}({\mathcal{B}}_{i})
. By induction,
{\mathcal{B}}_{i+1}
is a
G_{i}
-extension of
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
and
\operatorname{FPdim}({\mathcal{B}}_{i+1})=|G_{0}|\cdots|G_{i}|
. By definition (cf.
(
ENO2
, Sec.1)
),
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is a nilpotent category, and
\operatorname{FPdim}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})=|G_{0}|\cdots|G_{n-1}|
.
∎

7.2.
Fusion actions associated to
A_{n}

The simple roots for
A_{n}
can be expressed in terms of an orthonormal basis
e_{1},\ldots,e_{n+1}
as follows:

\alpha_{k}=e_{k}-e_{k+1}\text{ for }1\leq k\leq n.

Let
L
be the root lattice of type
A_{n}
and

\lambda_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}e_{j}-\frac{k}{n+1}\sum_{j=1}^{n+1}e_{j}

be the fundamental weights for
k=1,....,n
. Also set
\lambda_{0}=0.

Then
V_{L}
is a rational,
C_{2}
-cofinite VOA whose irreducible modules are
V_{L+\lambda_{r}}
for
0\leq r\leq n

D1993
.

By
(
DNR25
, Prop. 4.3)
that there exists another positive definite even lattice
K
such that the orthogonal sum
(L,K)=L+K
is a sublattice of an even unimodular lattice
E
and
E=\cup_{r=0}^{n}(L+\lambda_{r},K+\mu_{r})

where
K^{\circ}=\cup_{r=0}^{n}(K+\mu_{r})
is the dual lattice of
K
and
\mu_{0}=0.
Moreover,

{\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{rev}}_{V_{L}}}
is braided equivalent to
{\mathcal{M}_{V_{K}}}.

V_{E}
has two automorphisms
\sigma=\sigma_{E},\tau
such that

\tau
acts on
V_{L+\lambda_{r}}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}}
as scalar
e^{\frac{2\pi ir}{n+1}}
and
\sigma
is the involution
induced from the
-1
-isometry of
E.
Then
\tau,\sigma
generate a dihedral group
G
of order

2(n+1).
For each subspace
X
of
V_{E}
which is invariant under the action of
\sigma
, we have
X=X^{+}\oplus X^{-}
where
X^{\pm}
is the eigenspace of
\sigma
with eigenvalue
\pm 1.

Then
V_{E}^{G}=V_{L+K}^{+}=(V_{L}\otimes V_{K})^{+}.

In this example, we consider
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+}}}={\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{M}_{V_{K}^{+}}}
and the holomorphic VOA
A:=V_{E}
is a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}.
Note that
V_{L+K}^{+}
is also a condensable algebra in
{\mathcal{C}}
,
and
V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+}=(V_{L+K}^{+})^{\langle\sigma_{L}\rangle}.
Clearly,
V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}
is not a fixed point subalgebra of
V_{E}
under the action of any finite automorphism group of
V_{E}
, and so is
V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+}\subset V_{E}
.

7.2.1.
L=A_{2n}
cases

Recall that
\sigma_{L}
is the order 2 automorphism of
V_{L}
induced from the
-1
isometry of
L.
We denote the
\pm 1
-eigenspaces of
\sigma_{L}
on
V_{L}
by

V_{L}^{\pm}.

Then
V_{L+\lambda_{r}}\circ\sigma_{L}

DLM2000
is isomorphic to
V_{L-\lambda_{r}}=V_{L+\lambda_{2n+1-r}}
for
r=1,...,n.
It follows from
D1994
;
DLM2000
that
V_{L}
has a unique irreducible
\sigma_{L}
-twisted module
V_{L}^{T}
with the lowest weight
\frac{n}{8}

DLrt1996
. Then
\sigma_{L}
also acts on
V_{L}^{T}
such that the
\pm 1
-eigenspaces
(V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}
are irreducible
V_{L}^{+}
-modules. Furthermore, the fixed points
V_{L}^{+}
is a rational,
C_{2}
-cofinite VOA whose irreducible modules are

\{V_{L}^{\pm},\,V_{L+{\lambda_{r}}}\cong V_{L+\lambda_{2n+1-r}},\,(V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}\mid r=1,...,n\}.

The twists and dimensions of
V_{L}^{+}
-modules are as follows:

\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c}&V_{L}^{+}&V_{L}^{-}&V_{L+\lambda_{r}}&(V_{L}^{T})^{+}&(V_{L}^{T})^{-}\\
\hline\cr\theta&1&1&e^{\frac{r(2n+1-r)2\pi i}{2(2n+1)}}&e^{\frac{2\pi in}{8}}&-e^{\frac{2\pi in}{8}}\\
\hline\cr\dim&1&1&2&\sqrt{2n+1}&\sqrt{2n+1}\\
\end{array}

So
\dim{\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}}=8n+4.

Here are the fusion products of irreducible modules
ADL2005
for
i,j\neq 0
:

V_{L}^{-}{\otimes}V_{L+\lambda_{i}}=V_{L+\lambda_{i}},\quad V_{L}^{-}{\otimes}(V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}=(V_{L}^{T})^{\mp},

V_{L+\lambda_{i}}{\otimes}V_{L+\lambda_{j}}=V_{L+\lambda_{i}+\lambda_{j}}+V_{L+\lambda_{i}-\lambda_{j}}\ \ {\rm if}\ i\neq j,

V_{L+\lambda_{i}}{\otimes}V_{L+\lambda_{i}}=V_{L}^{+}+V_{L}^{-}+V_{L+2\lambda_{i}},

V_{L+\lambda_{i}}{\otimes}(V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}=(V_{L}^{T})^{+}+(V_{L}^{T})^{-},

(V_{L}^{T})^{+}{\otimes}(V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}=V_{L}^{\pm}+\sum_{r=1}^{n}V_{L+\lambda_{r}},

(V_{L}^{T})^{-}{\otimes}(V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}=V_{L}^{\mp}+\sum_{r=1}^{n}V_{L+\lambda_{r}}.

Let
d
be the rank of
K
. Again
V_{K}
has a unique irreducible
\sigma_{K}
-twisted module
V_{K}^{T}.

By
AD2004
, the irreducible modules of the rational,
C_{2}
-cofinite VOA
V_{K}^{+}

are

\{V_{K}^{\pm},\,V_{K+{\mu_{r}}}\cong V_{K+\mu_{2n+1-r}},\,(V_{K}^{T})^{\pm}\mid r=1,...n\}

with twists and dimensions:

\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c}&V_{K}^{+}&V_{K}^{-}&V_{K+\mu_{r}}&(V_{K}^{T})^{+}&(V_{K}^{T})^{-}\\
\hline\cr\theta&1&1&e^{\frac{-r(2n+1-r)2\pi i}{2(2n+1)}}&e^{\frac{2\pi id}{16}}&-e^{\frac{2\pi id}{16}}\\
\hline\cr\dim&1&1&2&\sqrt{2n+1}&\sqrt{2n+1}\\
\end{array}

So
\dim{\mathcal{M}_{V_{K}^{+}}}
is also equal to
8n+4.
The fusion rules of
V_{K}^{+}
-modules are the same as the fusion rules of
V_{L}^{+}
-modules with
L
replaced by
K
and
\lambda_{r}
by
\mu_{r}.

We believe that
{\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{rev}}_{V_{L}^{+}}}
is braided equivalent to
{\mathcal{M}_{V_{K}^{+}}},
but we cannot prove it in this paper.

First, note that
\dim{\mathcal{C}}=(8n+4)^{2}
and
A
has the decomposition in
{\mathcal{C}}
as follows:

A=V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+}\oplus V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{-}\oplus V_{L}^{-}\otimes V_{K}^{+}\oplus V_{L}^{-}\otimes V_{K}^{-}\oplus 2\bigoplus_{r=1}^{n}V_{L+\lambda_{r}}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}}\,.

It is now clear that
\dim A=\dim{\mathcal{C}}_{A}=8n+4
and

[A,A]_{{\mathcal{C}}}=4+4n.
Since
A
is holomorphic, we see that
|{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}|=4+4n
by Theorem
4.1
.

Next we determine the simple objects in
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}.
For
x\in{\mathcal{C}}
we denote the induced
A
-module by
\alpha(x).
Then for
r=1,...,n

\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}})=2\sum_{s=0}^{2n}V_{L+\lambda_{s}}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}+\mu_{s}}\quad\text{in }{\mathcal{C}},

and so

[\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}}),\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{s}})]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=2\delta_{r,s}.

Together with
A
itself, we have
2n+1
inequivalent simple objects in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}.
We denote this set by
H.
Clearly, each object in
H
has dimension
1.
From the fusion rules of
V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+}
-modules above we see that
H
is closed under the fusion product. So
H
is a group of order
2n+1.

One can easily see that

X=\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes(V_{K}^{T})^{+})\cong\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes(V_{K}^{T})^{-})=V_{L}\otimes V_{K}^{T}+2\sum_{r=1}^{n}V_{L+\lambda_{r}}\otimes V_{K}^{T}

and
Y=\alpha((V_{L}^{T})^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+})\cong\alpha((V_{L}^{T})^{-}\otimes V_{K}^{+})
are two new inequivalent simple objects in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
of dimensions

\sqrt{2n+1}.
Obviously, both
X
and
Y
are central elements in
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})=\overline{K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}.

One can also check that for any
x\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}}),

y\in\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}_{V_{K}^{+}}})

such that either
x\neq(V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}
or
y\neq(V_{K}^{T})^{\pm}
then
\alpha(x\otimes y)
is a sum of simple objects from
H\cup\{X,Y\}.

Since
|\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})|=4n+4,

|H\cup\{X,Y\}|=2n+3
, we see that the complement of

H\cup\{X,Y\}
in
\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
consists of
2n+1
invertible objects
of order
2.

Since every simple object in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
is a subobject of a induced module,
the remaining
2n+1
invertible objects have to be the subobjects of

\alpha((V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T})^{\pm})=(2n+1)(V_{L}^{T}\otimes V_{K}^{T}).

So
\alpha((V_{L}^{T})^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T})^{\pm})
is a direct sum of
2n+1
inequivalent invertible simple objects in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
of order
2.

By Lemma
7.2
, the group
G
of invertible objects of
{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}
contains the dihedral group
D_{2(2n+1)}
as
A^{G}=(V_{L}\otimes V_{K})^{+}.
Since
|{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}|=4n+4
,
G\cong D_{2(2n+1)}
.

Let
g\in G\setminus H.
Then

g\otimes_{A}X\cong Y,\ g\otimes_{A}Y\cong X

by using the relation

[g\otimes_{A}\alpha((V_{L}^{T})^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+}),\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes(V_{K}^{T})^{+})]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=[g,\alpha((V_{L}^{T})^{+}\otimes(V_{K}^{T})^{+})]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=1.

Similarly, if
g\in H
then

g\otimes_{A}X\cong X,\ g\otimes_{A}Y\cong Y.

So
H
is a normal subgroup of
G
isomorphic to cyclic group
\langle\tau\rangle
generated by
\tau.

Note that
{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}=G\cup\{X,Y\}.
Using the fusion rules for
V_{L}^{+}
and
V_{K}^{+}
we see fusion ring structure of
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
is as follows:

hX=X,hY=Y\ {\rm for}\ h\in H,gX=Y,gY=X\ {\rm for}\ g\in G\setminus H,

X^{2}=Y^{2}=\sum_{h\in H}h,\ XY=\sum_{g\in G\setminus H}g.

There are only two nontrivial fusion subcategories
{\mathcal{F}}_{X}=H\cup\{X\}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{Y}=H\cup\{Y\}
which are not pointed. One can verify that

V_{E}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{X}}=V_{L}\otimes V_{K}^{+}
and
V_{E}^{{\mathcal{F}}_{Y}}=V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}.

Since
D_{2(2n+1)}
has many subgroups in general, we only give the Galois correspondence for
n=1.
In this case,
D_{6}=S_{3}
which has 4 proper subgroups
H,H_{1},H_{2},H_{3}
of orders
3,2,2,2
where
H=\langle\tau\rangle,H_{1}=\langle\sigma\rangle,H_{2}=\langle\tau\sigma\rangle
and
H_{3}=\langle\tau^{2}\sigma\rangle.
One can easily find out that
V_{E}^{H}=V_{L+K},

V_{E}^{H_{1}}=V_{E}^{+},V_{E}^{H_{r}}=V_{L+K}^{+}+(V_{L+K+\lambda_{1}+\mu_{1}}+V_{L+K+\lambda_{2}+\mu_{2}})^{H_{r}}

for
r=2,3
where

(V_{L+K+\lambda_{1}+\mu_{1}}+V_{L+K+\lambda_{2}+\mu_{2}})^{H_{r}}=\{u+e^{\frac{-2\pi i(r-1)}{3}}\sigma(u)\mid u\in V_{L+K+\lambda_{1}+\mu_{1}}\}.

7.2.2.
A_{2n+1}
cases

Now only
V_{L}
and
V_{L+\lambda_{n+1}}
are
\sigma_{L}
-stable.
From
D1994
;
DLM2000
,
V_{L}
has two inequivalent irreducible
\sigma_{L}
-twisted module
V_{L}^{T_{i}}
for
i=1,2
with the lowest weight
\frac{2n+1}{16}

DLrt1996
. The
\sigma_{L}
also acts on
V_{L}^{T_{i}}
such that the
\pm 1
-eigenspaces
(V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{\pm}
are inequivalent irreducible
V_{L}^{+}
-modules. The irreducible
V_{L}^{+}
-modules are

\{V_{L}^{\pm},\,V_{L+{\lambda_{n+1}}}^{\pm},\,V_{L+{\lambda_{r}}}\cong V_{L+\lambda_{2n+2-r}},\,(V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{\pm}\mid r=1,...,n,i=1,2\}.

The twists and dimensions of
V_{L}^{+}
-modules are as follows:

\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c|c|c}&V_{L}^{\pm}&V_{L+\lambda_{n+1}}^{\pm}&V_{L+\lambda_{r}}&(V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{+}&(V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{-}\\
\hline\cr\theta&1&e^{\frac{(n+1)\pi i}{2}}&e^{\frac{r(2n+2-r)2\pi i}{2(2n+2)}}&e^{\frac{2\pi i(2n+1)}{16}}&-e^{\frac{2\pi i(2n+1)}{16}}\\
\hline\cr\dim&1&1&2&\sqrt{n+1}&\sqrt{n+1}\\
\end{array}

So
\dim{\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}}=8n+8.

We first discuss the fusion product of
({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}})_{V_{L}}
which is
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-graded such that

({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}})_{V_{L}}(1)=({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}})_{V_{L}}^{0}={\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}}}
and
({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}})_{V_{L}}(\sigma_{L})
is the
\sigma_{L}
-twisted module category. Observe that
{\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}}}
is pointed, and
\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}}})
is a cyclic group of order
2(n+1)
generated by
V_{L+\lambda_{1}}
. Since
({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}})_{V_{L}}(\sigma_{L})
is an indecomposable
{\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}}}
-module, we see that

\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}}})
acts on
\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}}})_{V_{L}}(\sigma_{L})=\{V_{L}^{T_{i}}\mid i=1,2\}
transitively. So

V_{L+\lambda_{r}}\otimes V_{L}^{T_{i}}=V_{L}^{T_{i+r}}
where
i+r
is understood as 1 or 2 modulo
2
and

V_{L}^{T_{i}}\otimes V_{L}^{T_{i}}=\sum_{r=0}^{n}V_{L+\lambda_{2r}},\quad V_{L}^{T_{1}}\otimes V_{L}^{T_{2}}=\sum_{r=0}^{n}V_{L+\lambda_{2r+1}}.

We also need the following fusion rules for some
V_{L}^{+}
-modules for
r=1,...,n,

i=1,2

ADL2005
:

V_{L}^{-}\otimes V_{L+\lambda_{r}}=V_{L+\lambda_{r}},\ V^{-}_{L}\otimes V_{L+\lambda_{n+1}}^{\pm}=V_{L+\lambda_{n+1}}^{\mp},\ V_{L}^{-}\otimes(V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{\pm}=(V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{\mp}.

The results above also hold for
V_{K}
with
\lambda_{r}
replaced by
\mu_{r}.

Again, let
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{M}_{V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+}}}
and
A=V_{E}.
Then
\dim{\mathcal{C}}=(8n+8)^{2}.
Using the decomposition of
A
in
{\mathcal{C}}

A=V_{L}\otimes V_{K}+V_{L+\lambda_{n+1}}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{n+1}}\oplus 2\bigoplus_{r=1}^{n}V_{L+\lambda_{r}}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}}

where
V_{L+\lambda}=V_{L+\lambda}^{+}+V_{L+\lambda}^{-}
for
\lambda=0,\lambda_{n+1}
and the same decompositions for
V_{K+\mu},

we see that

\dim A=\dim{\mathcal{C}}_{A}=8n+8,

[A,A]_{{\mathcal{C}}}=8+4n
and
{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}=8+4n.

Again,
V_{E}^{D_{2(2n+2)}}=(V_{L}\otimes V_{K})^{+}
and Lemma 7.2 applies. So
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
contains a pointed fusion subcategory
{\mathcal{B}}
such that
\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{B}})=D_{2(2n+2)}
. We still use
\tau
and
\sigma
to denote the generators, respectively order
2n+2
and
2
, of
D_{2(2n+2)}\subset\operatorname{Irr}({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})
.

Since
o(D_{2(2n+2)})=2(2n+2),
there are four more simple objects of
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
to be determined. We first determine
\alpha(x)
for any
x\in{\mathcal{C}}.

Then

\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}})=2\sum_{s=0}^{2n+1}V_{L+\lambda_{s}}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}+\mu_{s}}

for
r=1,...,n.
One can further show that
\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{r}})=\tau^{r}+\tau^{-r}
by using the explicit constructions of
\tau^{\pm r}
-twisted
V_{E}
-modules from
DM1994
.
Similarly,

\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{n+1}}^{\pm})=\sum_{s=0}^{2n+1}V_{L+\lambda_{s}}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{n+1}+\mu_{s}}=\tau^{n+1}

is simple of dimension
1.

Plus
A
itself, we have
2n+2
inequivalent simple objects in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}.
In fact, this set
H
of invertible objects is a cyclic group generated by
\tau.

One can easily see that

\alpha(V_{L}^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{i}})^{\pm})=\sum_{r=0}^{n}V_{L+{\lambda_{2r}}}\otimes V_{K}^{T_{i}}+\sum_{r=0}^{n}V_{L+\lambda_{2r+1}}\otimes V_{K}^{T_{i+1}}

are two new inequivalent simple objects in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
of dimensions

\sqrt{n+1}
for
i=1,2.
Similarly,

\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{\pm}\otimes V_{K}^{\pm})
give another two new inequivalent simple objects in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
of dimensions
\sqrt{n+1}.
Last four objects are central elements in
K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})=\overline{K({{\mathcal{C}}_{A}})}.

Since
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}
contains a pointed fusion subcategory whose irreducible objects form the dihedral group
D_{2(2n+2)}
, it remains to find another
2n+2
invertible objects of order
2.
One can compute that

\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{1}})^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{1}})^{\pm})=(n+1)(V_{L}^{T_{1}}\otimes V_{K}^{T_{1}})+(n+1)(V_{L}^{T_{2}}\otimes V_{K}^{T_{2}}),

\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{1}})^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{2}})^{\pm})=(n+1)(V_{L}^{T_{1}}\otimes V_{K}^{T_{2}})+(n+1)(V_{L}^{T_{2}}\otimes V_{K}^{T_{1}}),

[\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{j}})^{\pm}),\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{j}})^{\pm})]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=n+1,

[\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{1}})^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{1}})^{\pm}),\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{1}})^{\pm}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{2}})^{\pm})]_{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=0.

So both
\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{1}})^{+}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{1}})^{+})
and
\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{1}})^{+}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{2}})^{+})
are direct sum of
n+1
inequivalent simple objects in
{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}.
Together we have another

2(n+1)
invertible elements in
D_{2(2n+2)}.

For short we set

X_{i}=\alpha(V_{L}^{+}\otimes(V_{K}^{T_{i}})^{+}),\quad Y_{i}=\alpha((V_{L}^{T_{i}})^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+})

for
i=1,2.
Then

{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}=D_{2(2n+2)}\cup\{X_{i},Y_{i}|i=1,2\}

subject to relations

X_{i}^{2}=Y_{i}^{2}=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\tau^{2r},X_{1}X_{2}=Y_{1}Y_{2}=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\tau^{2r+1},

\tau X_{1}=X_{2},\tau X_{2}=X_{1},\tau Y_{1}=Y_{2},\tau Y_{2}=Y_{1},

X_{1}Y_{1}=Y_{1}X_{1}=X_{2}Y_{2}=Y_{2}X_{2}=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\tau^{2r}\sigma,

X_{1}Y_{2}=Y_{2}X_{1}=X_{2}Y_{1}=Y_{1}X_{2}=\sum_{r=0}^{n}\tau^{2r+1}\sigma.

The
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
has ten fusion subcategories which are not groups:

{\mathcal{F}}_{X_{i}}=\{\tau^{2r},X_{i}\mid r=0,...,n\},\ {\mathcal{F}}_{Y_{i}}=\{\tau^{2r},Y_{i}\mid r=0,...,n\},

{\mathcal{F}}_{X_{1},X_{2}}=H\cup\{X_{1},X_{2}\},\ {\mathcal{F}}_{Y_{1},Y_{2}}=H\cup\{Y_{1},Y_{2}\},

{\mathcal{F}}_{X_{1},Y_{1}}=D^{1}\cup\{X_{1},Y_{1}\},\ {\mathcal{F}}_{X_{2},Y_{2}}=D^{1}\cup\{X_{2},Y_{2}\},

{\mathcal{F}}_{X_{1},Y_{2}}=D^{2}\cup\{X_{1},Y_{2}\},\ {\mathcal{F}}_{X_{2},Y_{1}}=D^{2}\cup\{X_{2},Y_{1}\},

where

D^{1}=\langle\tau^{2},\sigma\rangle,D^{2}=\langle\tau^{2},\tau\sigma\rangle

are subgroups of
D_{2(2n+2)}
isomorphic to the dihedral group
D_{2n+2}.

One can verify that
the corresponding subalgebras of
V_{E}
are

V_{L}\otimes V_{K}^{+}+V_{L+\lambda_{n+1}}\otimes V_{\mu_{n+1}}^{\pm},\ V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}+V_{L+\lambda_{n+1}}^{\pm}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{n+1}},

V_{L}\otimes V_{K}^{+},\ V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K},V_{L}^{+}\otimes V_{K}^{+}+V_{L+\lambda_{n+1}}^{\pm}\otimes V_{K+\mu_{n+1}}^{\pm}.

7.3.
Fusion actions associated to coset constructions

Let
A
be a holomorphic vertex operator algebra and
U,V
be rational
C_{2}
-cofinite subalgebras
such that
U^{c}=V
and
V^{c}=U.
Then

A=\oplus_{i=0}^{p}U^{i}\otimes V^{i}

as
U\otimes V
-modules, where
\{U^{i}\mid i=0,...,p\}
are the inequivalent irreducible
U
-modules and

\{V^{i}\mid i=0,...,p\}
are the inequivalent irreducible
V
-modules with
U^{0}=U
and
V^{0}=V.

Moreover,
{\mathcal{M}_{U}}
and
{\mathcal{M}^{\operatorname{rev}}_{V}}
are equivalent (cf. Theorem 4.2 of
DNR25
). Let
{\mathcal{C}}={\mathcal{M}_{U\otimes V}}={\mathcal{M}_{U}}\boxtimes{\mathcal{M}_{V}}.
Then

{\operatorname{Irr}({\mathcal{C}}_{A})}=\{\alpha(U^{i}\otimes V)\mid i=0,...p\}

by Lemma 4.1 of
DRX
. So
K({\mathcal{C}}_{A})
is a commutative algebra whose irreducible characters
are given by
\chi_{i}
such that
\chi_{i}(\alpha(U^{j}\otimes V))=\frac{S^{U}_{i,j}}{\dim U^{i}}
for all
j
where
S^{U}=(S^{U}_{i,j})
is the
S
-matrix of
{\mathcal{M}_{U}}.
It is clear that

A^{{\mathcal{C}}_{A}}=U\otimes V.

Let
I=\{i\mid U^{i}\ {\rm is\ invertible}\}.
Then
B=\oplus_{i\in I}U^{i}\otimes V^{i}
is a subalgebra of
A
and
({\mathcal{C}}_{B}^{0})_{A}
is the full subcategory of
{\mathcal{C}}_{A}
generated by
\alpha(U^{j}\otimes V)
such that
S^{U}_{i,j}=\dim U^{j}
for all
i\in I.

Remark 7.4
.

In Section 3.3 of
Xu2014
, a list of all intermediate conformal nets for some examples of conformal inclusions were given by using Theorem 3.8 therein. These intermediate conformal nets can be recovered in the setting of VOAs by a similar approach with the currently developed Galois correspondence (Theorem
5.6
).
The same comments also apply to Theorem 3.14 of
Xu2013
, where a list of all intermediate VOAs for a class of conformal inclusions were presented.
